Required reserves in MDL maintained by banks during 8 December
2016 – 7 January 2017 accounted for MDL 10 063.7 million, up
by 26 percent compared with the reserves maintained by banks
during 8 December 2015 – 7 January 2016 (Chart 3.4).
The
signiﬁcant increase of required reserves in MDL occurred due
to the increase, during the analyzed period, of funds attracted by
banks in MDL and non-convertible currency.
Required reserves in FCC maintained by banks with the National
Bank accounted for USD 61.3 million and EUR 105.5 million as
at 31 December 2016 (Chart 3.5). Compared with 31 December
2015, required reserves both in USD and in EUR reduced by about
5.7 percent and 0.8 percent, respectively. This decrease occurred
due to the reduction in funds attracted in such foreign currencies,
to foreign currency deposit migration to MDL deposits.
Pursuant to Article 17(3) of Law on the National Bank of Moldova no.
548-XIII of 21 July 1995, in order to reduce the cost of bank assets,
the National Bank, for the share of required reserves exceeding
5.0 percent from the liabilities used to calculate these reserves,
paid to banks an interest, the amount of which was MDL 797.9
million, up by over 42.0 percent compared with 2015.
The
signiﬁcant
increase
in
the
NBM
expenses
from
the
remuneration of required reserves was driven by the increase in
amount of required reserves in MDL.
Intervention on the domestic foreign exchange market
In 2016, the National Bank of Moldova intervened on the domestic
foreign exchange market, mainly as a buyer of foreign currency,
to strengthen the ofﬁcial reserve assets.
Over the analyzed period, the volume of net transactions of the
National Bank of Moldova carried out on the interbank foreign
exchange market against MDL on the value date accounted for
USD 401.48 million, including foreign exchange conversions in the
form of purchases with the World Bank institutions in amount of
USD 3.78 million and purchase transactions with banks undergoing
windup in amount of USD 2.80 million (Chart 3.6).
Monetary policy communication and transparency
Over the year 2016, appreciating the institutional transparency
and communication role related to streamlining the monetary
policy aiming to reach the goal of ensuring and maintaining price
stability, the NBM kept on strengthening its position in the area
of monetary policy communication amid prompt and accurate
information of the general public.
The Inﬂation Report, published every quarter over the year 2016,
is still a core instrument of monetary policy communication,
enabling the general public to have a clear picture of the
analytical framework and reasoning that conﬁgured the monetary
policy across the reporting year. Besides the analysis of the most
recent macroeconomic and monetary evolutions, the inﬂation
forecast
on
an
eight-quarter
horizon
and
the
risks
and


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
39
uncertainties related to the achievement of the target inﬂation,
the summaries of the meetings of the Executive Board on the
promotion of the monetary policy, the Inﬂation Report consists of
technical boxes that disclose additional information about the
macroeconomic processes and phenomena, enabling the general
public to better understand the report and to form extensive
capabilities
to
perceive
the
interdependence
between
the
macroeconomic phenomena.
To ensure a better understanding of the quantitative value of
forecasted
inﬂation,
every
quarter
there
were
held
press
conferences for the presentation of the Inﬂation Report, attended
by the NBM management and mass-media representatives, during
which current and future evolutions of the foreign and domestic
economic
environment
were
covered,
and
the
risks
and
uncertainties that were taken into consideration when elaborating
the medium-term forecast.
Similar to previous years,
monetary policy decisions were
explained in press releases (12 in number), published on the day
of the ordinary meeting of the Executive Board of the NBM on the
promotion of monetary policy, envisaging the level of the base
rate and that of the minimum required reserves, as well as the
factors and prerequisites that are the root of these decisions.
At the same time, the NBM published press releases on inﬂation
evolution on a monthly basis, enclosing information about the
level of the inﬂation rate and its components, and the challenges
that the inﬂationary process faces while striving to maintain price
stability.
On the occasion of bank employee holiday, the National Bank
organized and hosted a range of ﬁnancial education activities and
an interactive seminar on the topic “Monetary Policy of the
National Bank of Moldova” in which second-year students of the
Agrarian University of the Republic of Moldova participated. These
activities were designated to increase the NBM transparency
related to the implementation and promotion of monetary policy,
thus contributing to a better understanding and comprehension of
monetary policy decisions and interdependence between various
macroeconomic processes from the Republic of Moldova.
The NBM’s website is the main monetary policy information
sharing tool; users can read, through an interactive database,
press
releases
on
monetary
policy
decisions,
current
and
forecasted inﬂation evolution, NBM publications (Annual Reports,
Inﬂation Reports), monetary market data in due time.
In 2016, the National Bank of Moldova answered to the requests
and claims sent in by the general public.
The topics of great
interest were the evolution of the base rate, average weighted
interest rate on credits and deposits granted to individuals in a
certain timeframe.
The NBM will continue to keep a transparent communication
system, capable to anchor inﬂationary anticipations in order to
streamline monetary policy in line with those ﬁve monetary policy
communication principles, such as transparency, coherence,
reciprocity,
opportunity and continuity,
set out in item XI
Monetary Policy Communication of the Medium-term monetary
policy strategy.


40
Annual Report (NBM, 2016)
Research, analysis and forecast
In 2016, there were carried out more studies and researches,
which aimed to develop and enhance the framework for the
analysis and forecast of the inﬂation and economic activity
necessary to substantiate the monetary policy decisions and to
implement effectively the inﬂation targeting strategy. The main
topics
addressed
within
the
studies
were
focused
on
the
assessment of aggregate demand impact on inﬂation, relationship
between industrial production prices and CPI, the impact of
exchange
rate
ﬂuctuations
on
the
core
inﬂation,
the
methodologies of price formation for energy products in the
Republic of Moldova and etc. Some of these studies delivered
important results and opinions that would facilitate the analysis
and forecast process, while others require additional efforts and
will be continued in the periods to come.
For example, the reassessment of aggregate demand impact on
inﬂation was attempted over the year. The results of the study
conﬁrm the assumption that a growth of economic activity above
the potential can exercise an inﬂationary effect on the prices
in the Republic of Moldova. However, the impact concerned is
much difﬁcult to identify due to signiﬁcant role of supply in the
inﬂationary process. To delimit the impact of supply factors from
the once determined by the excess of demand, the core inﬂation
was used instead of total inﬂation, considering that the former
excludes the inﬂuence of transitory effects. As a result, it was
found that within the sample analyzed in the time when core
inﬂation variation cannot be explained by the aggregate demand
deviation from the equilibrium value, this can usually be attributed
to exchange rate ﬂuctuations.
At the same time, it was established, both short and medium-
term, lack of correlations between the CPI evolution and industrial
production prices; this fact can be attributed mainly to different
structure of the two indicators and to the signiﬁcant weight of
goods imported on the domestic market.
Some
summaries
of
the
studies
conducted
during
2016,
addressing the factors or phenomena that had an impact on the
inﬂation evolution but also some technical aspects about CPI,
were published in the Inﬂation Reports as boxes. In this sense, the
Inﬂation Report no.1, February 2016, outlined the impact of
seasonal ﬂuctuations of prices on the inﬂation volatility in the
Republic of Moldova. Also, the Inﬂation Report no.3, August 2016,
discloses the advantages of annualizing data while analyzing the
inﬂation.
In 2016, the identiﬁcation and analysis of relevant factors for the
inﬂation process in the Republic of Moldova continued. In this
regard,
the
degree
of
inﬂuence
of
internal
and
external
macroeconomic
variables
on
the
growth
of
certain
subcomponents of the CPI have been re-estimated by means of
statistical
and
econometric
methods.
Subsequently,
this
information was used in the four rounds of forecasting for
achieving short-term inﬂation projections, key constituent of the
framework for the analysis and forecast of the inﬂation targeting
regime.
Additionally, in order to improve the accuracy of CPI


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
41
short-term forecast, during the reporting year, were further
monitored the prices of a basket of food products and non-food
products in the CPI in some commercial units in Chis, in˘au,
obtaining important operational data in time on price dynamics
and its possible causes. At the same time, the Inﬂation Reports
included assessments of short-term forecast deviation from actual
data, pointing out the main underlying objective factors.
At the same time, in order to identify and quantify the pressures
from aggregate demand on inﬂation during 2016, the activity for
estimating the economic activity and its potential level was also
pursued.
In this respect, the econometric equations for the
subcomponents of GDP uses were re-estimated, based on a
number of external and internal assumptions, such as the
economic activity of the major trading partners, the household
disposable income or the performance of agricultural sector.
The external vulnerabilities of the Republic of Moldova’s economy
had been studied during 2016.
The openness of the national
economy suggests a signiﬁcant degree of exposure to external
shocks,
which
explains
the
high
attention
to
risks
and
uncertainties related to the external environment within the
medium-term inﬂation forecasting.
At the same time, their
description in the Inﬂation Reports is an uncertainty presumption
of forecasted values and a warning on further international
economic events that must be monitored to anticipate the
evolution of external macroeconomic indicators with potential
impact on the inﬂation.
At the same time, detailed analyses of the external macroeconomic
situation were carried out, such as the alleviation of the growth
of emerging economies and the drop in international prices for
raw materials, as well as their outcome on the national economy.
After the referendum of the United Kingdom of Great Britain, there
were noticed increased volatility of stock exchange indicators,
withdrawal of capital from Great Britain by foreign investors and
considerable depreciation of the Pound sterling. This event, with
major impact on the global economy, was thoroughly analyzed to
identify possible Brexit effects on the national economy.


42
Annual Report (NBM, 2016)
3.2
The
results
of
monetary
and
foreign exchange policy
In early 2016, there were prerequisites for the reversal of inﬂation’s
2015 upward trend. The monetary policy conduct was affected
by the complexity of the balance of risks with the prevalence of
disinﬂationary ones.
The disinﬂationary process was driven by weak economic activity
from euro area countries and ongoing recession in the Russian
Federation – the main foreign trading partners of the Republic of
Moldova. Domestic macroeconomic indicators’ dynamic showed
positive, but shy, signals in terms of economic activity revival.
Having analyzed the balance of risks, it was anticipated a gradual
reduction process of the annual rate of growth in prices, including
due to high comparison base of 2015.
Thus, in January 2016, the NBM upheld the base rate applied on
the main short-term monetary policy operations at 19.5 percent
annually. Taking into consideration the inﬂation evolution forecasts,
the previous monetary policy decisions and the speed with which
these decisions spread across the economy, the Executive Board
of the NBM, during the meetings of February-July 2016, decided
to reduce the monetary policy interest rate from 19.5 percent to
10.0 percent annually.
In August 2016, the deceleration of the annual rate of inﬂation with
the prevalence of disinﬂationary risks in line with the forecasts
of the National Bank of Moldova, as well as the high uncertainty
related to the forecast, determined the Executive Board to uphold
the base rate applied on main monetary policy operations at the
same level of 10.0 percent.
In September-October 2016,
the gradual calibration of the
monetary policy conduct envisaged to ensure real monetary
conditions for lending and savings activities, as well as to boost
domestic demand amid further adjustment of the domestic
economic environment to the volatilities and uncertainties of the
external one.
In this regard, the National Bank of Moldova
decreased the monetary policy interest rate twice in a row, by 0.5
percentage points, to the level of 9.0 percent annually.
Until late 2016, the base rate was upheld at the same level of 9.0
percent; the decisions of the Executive Board of the NBM based
on the fact that the monetary policy measures taken by the NBM
in early 2016 had to be transposed into the national economy
through various channels, including by inﬂuencing the interest
rates on loans and deposits in national currency, thus, continuing
to exercise effects on inﬂation development.
In order to adequately manage and sterilize liquidity excess
accrued during 2016 and to improve the transmission mechanism
of monetary policy decisions, in the context of ensuring an
appropriate
set
of
monetary
conditions
aimed
to
achieve
convergence of inﬂation rate to the target in the medium-term,
the National Bank of Moldova upheld the minimum required
reserves
ratio
from
the
means
attracted
in
MDL
and


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
43
non-convertible currency at the level of 35.0 percent. At the same
time, the minimum required reserve ratio from means attracted in
freely convertible currency was upheld at the level of 14.0 percent
during 2016.
These decisions aimed to anchor inﬂationary expectations in the
contest of maintaining the inﬂation rate within the variation range
of ± 1.5 percentage points of the target of 5.0 percent.
Dynamic of monetary indicators
During 2016, monetary indicators recorded an upward trend.
Thus, the average growth rate of money supply accounted for 2.8
percent in annual terms.
The development of all monetary
aggregates was similar during the reference year.
Money supply
In January-December 2016, money supply (M213) increased by
18.3 percent, by 27.4 percentage points above the level of annual
growth in 2015 (Chart 3.7). Starting with the second quarter of
2016, monetary aggregate M2 was positive, showing an upward
trend for the entire year. Both components of the money supply M2
recorded positive evolutions. Thus, deposits in national currency
and money in circulation increased compared with 2015 by 22.8
percent or by MDL 5 403.9 million and by 11.4 percent or by MDL
1 764.9 million, respectively.

The evolution of deposits in MDL recorded a pronounced increase,
reaching the maximum of 27.3 percent annually in October;
however, in the last two months of the year, it reversed its
upward trend accounting, at the end of the year, for 22.8 percent
annually.
During the entire year, the evolution of money in
circulation saw an upward trend, the growth rate being by 22.8
percentage points higher than in 2015.
At the same time, money supply (M314) recorded an annual growth
of 10.2 percent, the growth rate being by 13.2 percentage points
higher than in 2015 (Chart 3.8).
In late 2016, the growth rate of deposits in foreign currency
accounted for -2.6 percent.
Term deposits in foreign currency
recalculated in MDL recorded a drop of MDL 1 125.3 million, or by
6.5 percent annually, while the sight deposits increased by MDL
469.9 million or by 6.2 percent.
In late 2016, the share of deposits in MDL in total of deposits
accounted for 54.8 percent, while the share of deposits in foreign
currency – 45.2 percent, changing during the reference year by
5.8 percentage points in favor of deposits in MDL.
The share of term deposits in total deposits held 62.9 percent in
the structure of deposits in MDL at the end of December 2016,
decreasing by 3.2 percentage points compared with late December
2015.

of residents expressed in MDL.


44
Annual Report (NBM, 2016)
The total volume of new term deposits attracted in national
currency recorded an increase of MDL 5 570.3 million or by 21.0
percent.
This was due to the increase of new term deposits
attracted in national currency from individuals, which was up by
MDL 5 324.1 million or by 28.4 percent compared with 2015, and
due to the increase of new deposits attracted in national currency
from legal entities by MDL 246.2 million compared with 2015 (3.1
percent).
During 2016, the volume of term deposits in foreign currency
recalculated in MDL decreased by MDL 3 073.5 million or by 16.8
percent compared with 2015, due to the decrease in volume of
new deposits attracted in foreign currency from individuals by MDL
1 977.3 million (-13.4 percent) and those from legal entities by
MDL 1 096.3 million (-31.3 percent).
Lending market
During 2016, the balance of credits granted to economy dropped,
while credits granted to legal entities recorded a negative annual
dynamic.
Thus, in late 2016, the annual decrease rate of the
total balance of credits granted to economy was of 7.6 percent,
compared with the growth of 3.5 percent recorded in late 2015
(Chart 3.9).
This decrease was driven both by the evolution
of component in national currency (-10.6 percent) and foreign
currency (-3.8 percent).

Evolution of new granted loans
In 2016, the annual volume of new granted loans had a downward
trend, accounting for MDL 22 465.7 million, by 11.6 percent less
than in 2015. The annual decline was even on both components,
the volume of loans in national currency decreased to MDL 12
862.0 million (Chart 3.10), and the loans in foreign currency – to
MDL 9 603.7 million (Chart 3.11).
The annual volume of loans in national currency granted to legal
entities decreased by 26.6 percent, to MDL 8 410.3 million,
accounting for 65.4 percent of total loans in MDL. At the same
time, the demand of loans to individuals saw an upward trend,
their share in the total loans being of 34.6 percent. Thus, the
annual volume of loans in national currency granted to population
during 2016 was of MDL 4 451.8 million, by 19.5 percent more
than in 2015.
Loans in foreign currency granted to legal entities, making up a
share of 98.8 percent of the total loans in foreign currency, reduced
by 6.0 percent, to MDL 9 490.2 million, while the volume of loans
granted to individuals (the share of which made up 1.2 percent


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
45
of the total loans in foreign currency) decreased by 16.5 percent,
reaching MDL 113.5 million.
Interest rates

rate applied to the main monetary policy operations 7 times.

The average interest rate of deposits attracted in foreign currency
recorded a downward trend, and at the end of the year reached a
minimum annual level of 2.03 percent, lower by 0.09 percentage
points than the level of December 2015 (Chart 3.14). The weighted
average rate of deposits attracted in foreign currency accounted
for 2.12 percent annually, lower by 0.44 percentage points than
in 2015.
This downward trend, over the course of 2016, was
characteristic for both segments of clients: the average rate of
deposits of legal entities made up 1.93 percent, by 0.74 percentage
points lower than in 2015, and the rate of deposits placed by
individuals summed up 2.16 percent, or by 0.37 percentage points
lower than in 2015.
In 2016, as a result of the developments of the average interest
rates of deposits attracted and loans granted in MDL, as well as
the downward rhythm of the average rate of deposits in national
currency being more pronounced than that of loans, the banking
margin in national currency increased by 1.53 percentage points,
compared with 2015, and recorded a value of 3.49 percent. The
banking margin, for foreign currency operations, recorded an
annual decrease by 0.67 percentage points, reaching the level of
3.78 percent (Chart 3.15).


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
47
3.3
Money market
Primary market of state securities
Acting as the ﬁscal agent of the state in organizing the placement
and redemption of state securities (SS) in book-entry form, the
National Bank of Moldova carried out 171 auctions of SS placement
during 2016.
In the speciﬁc circumstances of 2016 and following the need to
increase the budget deﬁcit funding at the expense of domestic
sources, the Ministry of Finance made, over the year, a number of
changes in volumes and types of SS proposed for sale. Thus, amid
constraints generated by the uncertainties that shadowed the
issuance of external funding instalments, the Ministry of Finance
increased, in the ﬁrst and second quarters, the volume of treasury
bills (T-bills) on all maturities announced weekly for placement
through auctions. In the second half of the year, considering the
forecasted ﬂow of external funding targeted mostly as budget
assistance from the International Monetary Fund, the European
Commission, the World Bank institutions, as a result of the signing
of a new Memorandum of economic and ﬁnancial policies between
the Republic of Moldova and IMF, the Ministry of Finance decreased
gradually the volumes announced at the auctions.
Government bonds (GB) with the maturity of 2 years were put
up for sale during the auctions carried out every month during
the year, in the amount similar to the one of 2015 – MDL 15.0
million. The intent of the Ministry of Finance to increase the SS
average maturity period and to boost interest of investors for SS
with longer maturity, led to the resumption, in the last quarter of
year, of issuance of bonds with ﬁxed interest rate and maturity of
3 years.

The evolution of interest rates on the primary market of state
securities reﬂected the combined inﬂuences exercised during 2016
by the persistence of liquidity excess in the banking sector, the
decrease of the NBM monetary policy rate, the gradual decrease
of the volume announced for placement by the issuing authority.
Thus, the state securities interest rates’ growth slowed down in
the beginning of the year, followed by a downward adjustment
in the following months, however, at different pace, being more
pronounced in April-May (Chart 3.17).
The higher interest rates for SS compared with the interest rates
offered by banks for deposits increased non-bank investors’


48
Annual Report (NBM, 2016)
interest for SS. Thus, the amount of SS purchased by non-bank
investors grew compared with the previous year in absolute terms,
recording MDL 2 145.8 million or 19.1 percent of the total awarded
volume. The balance of SS purchased by non-residents summed
up MDL 14.6 million compared with MDL 2.9 million in 2015.

These evolutions were accompanied by the occurrence of some
favorable changes in the structure of newly issued state securities:
the issuance of treasury bills with 21-day maturity ceased and the
share of T-bills with 91 and 182-day maturity reduced in favor of
bills with 364-day maturity and government bonds with the term
of circulation of 2 and 3 years (Chart 3.18 and 3.19). In these
conditions, the average maturity of issued securities recorded a
broad expansion (from 211 to 234 days).
The analysis of interest rates evolution on state securities
breakdown by maturity shows a moderate volatility, but the
deviation between them decreased in the ﬁrst decade of March;
thus, the interest rates on the maturity range were closer in value
and preserved this trend until the end of the year (Chart 3.20). At
the last placement auctions of SS of 2016, the average nominal
interest rates recorded values of 5.16, 5.59 and 5.47 percent
annually for SS with maturities of 91,
182 and 364 days
respectively, recording downward adjustments as compared with
the values recorded in late 2015.
The annual average interest rate of SS with the maturity up to one
year, awarded in 2016, recorded 15.81 percent annually compared
with 20.14 percent annually in 2015.

In the ﬁrst four months of 2016, the interest rates on SS maintained
leadership position in the yield hierarchy on ﬁnancial market.
Further, recording a signiﬁcant decrease, dropped in the corridor
formed by interest rates on granted loans and deposits attracted
in the economy, balancing within it until almost the end of the
year, when they placed below interest rates on attracted deposits.
At the same time, the gap between the interest rates on granted
loans and attracted deposits recorded a sensitive increase, amid
the reduction of the latter (Chart 3.21).
The volume of state securities placed on the primary market
through auctions, in circulation at 31 December 2016, amounted
to MDL 6 804.8 million at nominal value (Chart 3.22), at sale-
purchase price, these securities accounted for MDL 6 115.0 million,
increasing by MDL 953.0 million compared with the end of 2015. As
for the SS in circulation at 31 December 2016, those with residual
maturity up to three months accounted for 39.9 percent, from 3
to 6 months – 28.9 percent, from 6 to 12 months – 28.9 percent,
from one to two years – 1.9 percent and from two to three years –
0.4 percent.

Overall 2016, the secondary market of state securities was marked

Record-keeping of securities in the book-entry system of
the NBM
The securities book-entry system (BES) is a state securities and
the NBM certiﬁcates storage and settlement system. The system
enables the processing of transfer orders in real time and the ﬁnal
and irrevocable settlement of transactions with securities from this
system.
The system processes operations in relations with the issuer
(primary market, payment of interest, redemption), secondary
market operations (buy-sell, repo, transfer of portfolio), monetary
market operation carried out by the NBM.
In late 2016, 14 participants, including 11 banks, the National
Bank, the Ministry of Finance, and the Deposits Guarantee Fund in
the banking system were recorded in BES.

On foreign exchange transfer market, the resident banks traded
foreign currency against MDL in a volume reduced by USD 1 444.4
million (84.2 percent), legal entities – by USD 341.2 million (5.6
percent), the National Bank of Moldova – by USD 200.8 million
(30.7 percent) compared to 2015.
On the foreign exchange cash market, the turnover of foreign
exchange bureau of the licensed banks dropped by 3.8 percent,
while that of foreign exchange ofﬁces increased by 3.1 percent.
In 2016, the USD continued to prevail in the structure of total
turnover of the transaction on the domestic foreign exchange
market, although it lost positions against EUR – 6.8 percentage
points, up to 48.3 percent (charts 3.32 and 3.33). In the structure
of turnover of the foreign exchange cash market, the European
single currency held the largest share (Table A.10).
In 2016, purchases of foreign currency against national currency,
conducted by licensed banks on the domestic foreign exchange
market tightened by USD 1 163.1 million, or by 19.4 percent
compared with 2015 (Table A.9). This decrease was due to the
decrease in purchases of foreign currency against MDL from the
resident banks by USD 722.2 million (84.2 percent), amid reduction
16The amount does not include purchases/sales against MDL carried out between
the NBM and the Ministry of Finance.


54
Annual Report (NBM, 2016)
of foreign currency swap operations, and from the NBM – by USD
453.3 million (94.1 percent).
At the same time, the purchases of foreign currency from
individuals (including those from foreign exchange ofﬁces17)
increased by USD 120.5 million (5.7 percent).
In 2016, licensed banks purchased foreign currency against MDL
on the domestic foreign exchange market mostly from individuals
(including from foreign exchange ofﬁces17) – 46.3 percent, from
legal entities – 42.8 percent, from resident banks – 2.8 percent.
In 2016, sales of foreign currency against MDL carried out by
licensed banks of the Republic of Moldova on the domestic foreign
exchange market decreased by USD 1 022.0 million or by 17.5
percent,
compared with 2015.
This decrease was mainly
determined by the decrease in sales of foreign currency against
MDL to resident banks – by USD 722.2 million (84.2 percent), amid
foreign exchange swap operations reduction. Also, the sales of
foreign currency to legal entities decreased by USD 277.7 million
(7.1 percent) and to individuals18 – by USD 173.5 million (37.2
percent).
At the same time, the sales of foreign currency against MDL,
carried out by domestic banks, to the NBM increased by 2.5 times,
compared with 2015.
In 2016, licensed banks sold foreign currency on the domestic
market predominantly to legal entities – 75.6 percent, the NBM
– 8.8 percent, individuals – 7.9 percent and resident banks – 2.8
percent.
In these circumstances, local banks recorded net sales of foreign
currency to legal entities in amount of USD 1 576.0 million in 2016,
which were covered in excess with the net purchases of foreign
currency from individuals (including from foreign exchange ofﬁces)
– USD 1 861.2 million. The NBM absorbed foreign currency excess
on the foreign exchange market through net purchases of USD
397.7 million (Table A.11).
The dynamic of foreign exchange assets and liabilities of
the licensed banks
During 2016,
the balance-sheet assets in foreign currency
decreased due to net repayment of loans carried out by the
domestic banks in favor of foreign funders and individuals’
savings in national currency (especially during the ﬁrst 9 months).
Thus, the share of balance-sheet assets reduced by 5.2 percentage
points, to 38.9 percent in total assets in the banking sector, while
the share of balance-sheet liabilities – by 6.0 percentage points, to
46.8 percent in total debts across the banking system.
In these circumstances, as at 31 December 2016, the balance of
balance-sheet foreign exchange assets19 of licensed banks (loans

Over 2016, the lending activity in foreign currency continued to
shrink (Chart 3.34). At the end of the reference year, the balance of
loans granted in foreign currency by the domestic banks summed
up USD 759.0 million, by USD 47.6 million (5.9 percent) less than in
2015. By excluding exchange rate differences, the drop in balance
of loans was smaller, of USD 27.2 million (Table A.12).
According to the situation of 31 December 2016, the balance of
loans in EUR in the foreign currency structure of granted loans was
of 62.6 percent, the difference of 37.4 percent being attributed
to loans granted in USD (Table A.13). In 2016, lending evolved
in opposite directions, depending on the type of foreign currency.
In this regard, the balance of loans in EUR against the actual
exchange rate accounted for USD 475.4 million, slightly increasing
(by 0.3 percent). By excluding exchange rate differences, the
balance of loans granted in EUR increased signiﬁcantly by USD
22.0 million (4.9 percent). At the same time, the balance of loans
granted in USD decreased by USD 49.4 million (14.8 percent), up
to USD 283.6 million.
During 2016, the share of disposable funds in foreign currency in
total balance-sheet assets in foreign currency varied from 29.9 to
35.3 percent and at the end of the year made up 35.0 percent,
thus, the banks had more than a comfortable level of liquidity
in foreign currency to satisfy uninterrupted current settlement
procedures.
In late 2016, the balance of disposable funds in foreign currency
recorded by domestic banks was USD 497 million, decreasing by
USD 26.320 million (by 5.0 percent) compared with 31 December
2015. According to the structure, all types of liquidity decreased,
except cash in foreign currency, which increased by 26.3 percent
(Table A.14).
The balance of disposable funds in foreign currency of licensed
banks, as at 31 December 2016, had the structure as follows:
“Nostro” accounts opened abroad – 63.5 percent, cash in foreign
currency – 19.6 percent, placements abroad – 10.3 percent,
overnight
placements
–
6.4
percent
and
foreign
currency
securities – 0.2 percent.
As to the foreign currency structure of disposable funds of domestic
banks as at 31 December 2016, the largest share was held by the
European single currency – 56.9 percent, followed by the USD –
20Of which USD 10.8 million were due to the exchange rate difference.

The share of disposable funds in EUR increased by 2.7 percentage

compared with 31 December 2015.
As at 31 December 2016, the balance of required reserves in
foreign currency did not change too much compared with the end
of 2015, accounting for USD 171.7 million (down by USD 9.4 million

in foreign currency of licensed banks, balancing around this value
during the entire reference period.
The balance of foreign currency-linked assets accounted for USD

granted foreign currency-linked loans accounting for USD 55.3

assets.

less pronounced – of USD 88.6 million (5.9 percent).

3.35).

currency of licensed banks (term deposits, various deposits and
current accounts of clients, except for those opened by other
banks) decreased by the equivalent of USD 63.0 million or by 4.7

57
foreign currency of individuals accounted for USD 875.7 million,
dropping by USD 76.1 million compared with the end of 2015,
while by excluding the exchange rate differences, it was found
that the decrease was lower, of USD 56.5 million.
The balance of other types of accounts in foreign currency of clients
(current accounts and sight deposits) in late 2016 summed up the
equivalent of USD 575.6 million, recording a slight increase of USD
6.9 million (1.2 percent), while by excluding the exchange rate

main balance-sheet liabilities in foreign currency accounted for 5.1

As to the foreign currency structure of main balance-sheet

strengthened its position, accounting at the end of the year for

recorded an opposite dynamic, the balance of current accounts
and deposits in EUR increased by 2.5 percent.
On 31 December 2016, the share of current accounts and deposits
in USD of clients accounted for 35.1 percent, decreasing by 1.9
percentage points compared with the end of 2015. During 2016,
deposits in USD recorded larger shares, of 38.8 percent. At the
end of the reporting period, the balance of current accounts and
deposits in USD summed up USD 445.8 million, dropping by USD

Current accounts and deposit accounts in Russian rubles and
other foreign currency continued to hold a small share in the

the banking system (Table A.17).
In late 2016, the balance of conditional assets and liabilities in
foreign currency of licensed banks decreased by 30.6 percent
and 30.3 percent, respectively, compared with 31 December
2015 (Table A.18). In late 2016, conditional assets and liabilities
in foreign currency recorded almost the same values, conﬁrms
domestic banks’ commitments to achieve conversions of foreign
currency/foreign currency as many as possible.
As to the total open foreign currency position (long plus short)
recorded by the banking system in late 2016, it increased by USD
3.7 million and accounted for 1.75 percent in total regulatory
capital. The position opened in EUR, equivalent of USD 6.6 million
improved, from -1.43 percent to -0.02 percent in the total
regulatory capital.
At the same time, a slow increase was
recorded, generated by the decrease of the position in USD by
USD 1.5 million, from 1.97 percent to 1.59 percent in the total
regulatory capital, as well as the position in other foreign
currencies (especially Russian ruble).

In 2016, a more favorable situation was recorded on the domestic
foreign currency market compared with 2015, triggered by the
improvement of the trading balance and net supply of foreign
currency from individuals. Trading deﬁcit tightened as a result of
the increase in export of goods (+4.0 percent) in a larger share
than imports (+0.8 percent). Despite the decrease of transfers
from abroad in favor of individuals by 4.4 percent, net supply
of foreign currency from the latter, especially the one in EUR,
recovered amid the dedollarization phenomenon (notably in the
ﬁrst three quarters of the year). In this context, net EUR supply
increased in the foreign currency structure of net foreign currency
supply by 7.4 percentage points compared with 2015, amounting
to 62.8 percent, followed by the USD (23.1 percent) and the
Russian ruble, the share of which has reduced twice, accounting
for 12.4 percent (Chart 3.40).
In these circumstances, net demand of foreign currency from
economic agents recorded a downward trend for the third year in a
row, decreasing by 12.0 percent, to USD 1 576 million, being ﬁlled
120.1 percent from net supply of foreign currency from individuals,
which increased by 21.2 percent, up to USD 1 892.6 million.

These evolutions created prerequisites required for the NBM

In these circumstances, from an exchange rate of MDL/USD
20.0737 in the ﬁrst quarter, decreasing by 0.8 percent compared

of MDL by 5.3 percent in the ﬁrst 2 weeks of the year, amid

placed at MDL/USD 19.7957 in the second quarter, also balancing
near similar in the third quarter (average MDL/USD 19.7896).
Unlike the previous periods, in the fourth quarter of 2016, the gap
between the demand and supply of foreign currency broadened on
the foreign exchange market, signaling a lack of USD 45.6 million.
In this period, net supply of foreign currency from individuals
dropped by 39.4 percent to USD 351.4 million (a level similar to
the one recorded in the fourth quarter of 2015), although in the
previous quarters net supply was increasing. Besides the cut-down
in transfers from abroad in favor of individuals (by 2.4 percent)
and the speciﬁc evolution during this season, the decrease in
supply was due, to a certain extent, to the depreciation of EUR on
the external markets (from 1.13 EUR/USD at the beginning of the
quarter to 1.05 EUR/USD at the end). As a result, demand of foreign
currency by individuals intensiﬁed in the fourth quarter of 2016
and it was noticed their preference for hoarding in foreign currency,
thus the balance of their accounts in foreign currency increasing by
about USD 13 million24 (taking into consideration that the previous
periods recorded a deposits’ dedollarization process).
As to the net demand of foreign currency from economic agents, it
slightly increased by 2.1 percent, up to USD 397.0 million in the

Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
61
Management of foreign exchange reserves
Pursuant to Article 5, 16 and 53 of the Law on the National Bank
of Moldova, the NBM holds and manages foreign exchange
reserves of the State, performs foreign exchange operations using
the foreign exchange reserves and maintains them at a level
appropriate for implementing the monetary and foreign exchange
policy of the State. The role of foreign reserves is to ensure the
ﬁnancial credibility and stability of the country, honor payments
related to external obligations of the State and cope with
unforeseen exogenous shocks.
In the management of foreign reserves, the NBM ensures a high
degree of safety and a necessary level of liquidity of investment.
The NBM investment policy is prudent, seeking to optimize the
proﬁtability, provided that the liquidity and investment security
is ensured.
An essential element of the process of managing
foreign exchange reserves is the management of investment risk,
which is achieved by imposing constraints and limits related to
investments.
Table A.19 shows the main risks related to the management of
foreign exchange reserves and the methods used to reduce them.
Under the cooperation with the World Bank, the Strategic Asset
Allocation (SAA) is implemented since July 2013. Strategic asset
allocation involves a vision of long-term asset management aimed
to achieve the optimal level of return and risk. In the SAA context,
international reserves are divided in three installments (current,
liquidity and investment) depending on the speciﬁc objectives
and regulations, this approach ensuring a more effective objective
achievement.
The portfolio of securities held to maturity and investments in gold
are part of the investment installment.
Thereafter, the world economy and the decisions of the monetary
authorities of the USA, European Union and Great Britain will be
analyzed, which have had an impact on the NBM foreign exchange
reserves management process.
2016 was marked by geopolitical risks, terrorist attacks in France,
Belgium
and
Germany,
violent
riots
in
the
USA,
extreme
meteorological phenomena that led to high vulnerability of
economy worldwide. The world economy was affected mostly by
two events: Brexit and the results of the presidential elections in
the USA.
At the same time, this year was a fragile period for the world
economy.
Almost
inert
international
trade
and
discrete
investments were some of the main factors that slowed down
world economy growth.
As
to
the
euro
area,
the
European
Central
Bank
took
unprecedented measures during the reference period to stimulate
economy growth and to boost the inﬂation. In this sense, on 10
March 2016, the Governing Council of ECB decided to adopt a set
of measures to achieve the objective on price stability. Thus, the
interest rate on main reﬁnancing operations of the Euro system
was reduced by 5 basis points, to 0.00 percent; the interest rate
on marginal lending facility was reduced by 5 basis points, to 0.25


62
Annual Report (NBM, 2016)
percent; the interest rate on deposit facility was reduced by 10
basis points to -0.40 percent.
In terms of unconventional monetary policy measures, starting
with April, the volume of monthly operations carried out within
the program of purchase of assets increased from EUR 60 billion
to EUR 80 billion. This purchase was planned to be performed
until the end of March 2017 or, if required, until a sustainable
adjustment of the inﬂation trajectory was noticed, which would be
in line with the inﬂation-related objective. Also, starting with June,
long-term reﬁnancing (LTRO II) and purchase operations started
within the program of purchase of bonds issued by the corporate
sector.
However, despite low interest rates and a broad program of
issuance of electronic currency, the inﬂation stayed far from the
targeted value, recording an annual growth rate of only 0.2
percent.
During 2016, the Monetary Policy Committee of the Bank of
England established its monetary policy to achieve the inﬂation
target of 2 percent in a way that would contribute to economy
growth and employement. In this sense, on August meeting was
adopted a set of measures which would contribute to economy
growth and inﬂation’s durable return to the target. This package
of measures included: a reduction of the base rate by 25 basis
points to the level of 0.25 percent; a new term ﬁnancing scheme
to strengthen the base rate transmission mecanism; purchase of
corporate bonds from Great Britain in volume of up to GBP 10
billion;
and
a
broader
scheme
of
purchase
of
assets
for
government bonds from Great Britain of GBP 60 billion, up to the
total level of such assets purchase of GBP 435 billion.
However, the event that marked Great Britain’s economy the most
was the Brexit referendum of 23 June 2016, in which the citizens
decided that their country leave the European Union. According to
ﬁnal results, 51.9 percent of voters voted for Brexit. The turnout
was 72.2 percent.
The result of the referendum was a surprise, generating turbulence
on the international ﬁnancial markets, with implications for the
global economic development, due to uncertainties, including
political ones.
However, defying the widely spread forecasts on the surprise
decision of Great Britain to leave the EU, its economy continued to
grow
steadily.
It
is
also
considered
that
the
economic
disadvantages of the Brexit had not been yet felt in their full
power, that the aftermath was postponed rather than avoided.
An obvious adverse reaction to the results of the referendum was
the severe depreciation of the pound sterling, which led to further
inﬂation growth and reduction of disposable income of consumers.
The pound sterling recorded the lowest decrease against the USD
from the last three decades.
The increase of interest rates in the USA in December led to a
greater depreciation of the pound against the USD. Thus, the GBP
depreciated against the USD by about 17 percent right after the
Brexit. At the same time, the weak pound encouraged foreign


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
63
investments in shares and other assets denominated in this
currency.
Great Britain’s GDP recorded, in 2016, an annual increase of
1.8 percent, down by 0.2 percentage points compared with the
forecasted level and lower than the one recorded in 2015, of
2.2 percent. The growth rate stopped due to lower investments
(amid uncertainties related to future trading relationships between
Great Britain and the European Union), fact that can affect further
economic performance of this country. As a result, Great Britain
lost the title of economy with the highest growth rate among G7
member states, ceding it to Germany, which recorded an annual
increase of 1.9 percent.
During 2016, the USA aimed to reduce unemployment rate and
to stabilize prices. The gross domestic product increased by 1.6
percent, a lower increase rate than the one recorded in 2015 (2.6
percent) and the lowest in the last 5 years on record. At the same
time, workforce market continued to improve, being characterized
by the creation of new jobs and the unemployment rate recording
a drop from 5.3 percent in 2015 to 4.8 percent in 2016.
The
inﬂation rate balanced, during the ﬁrst 9 months of the year, at
the level of 1 percent, situating below the medium-term target
level of 2 percent, but in the fourth quarter CPI reached the level
of 1.8 percent.
To the end of the reference year, both indicators targeted by the
Federal Reserve System, such as employment and inﬂation were
close to the estimated values, which initially led to increased
likelihood of the growth of the reference rate, and further, to
making it possible pursuant to the Decision of the Federal Open
Market Committee of 14 December 2016 to increase the rate by
0.25 percent.
During the presidential elections of the USA of 8 November, the

victory was awarded to the representative of the republican party.
Thus, the second surprise of the year, after the Brexit, occurred.
The elections results determined the growth of ﬁnancial market
volatility and uncertainties about new policies that were to be
implemented.
Unstable global political situation contributed to the increase
of demand of gold (seen as refuge asset during hard times),
thus determining its appreciation during 2016. The appetite for
investments in gold was also triggered by the low interest rate in
the major world countries.
World economy evolution trend is also seen in the evolution of
proﬁtability rates (Chart 3.43). The evolution of base rates set by
the monetary authorities can be seen in chart 3.44.
On 31 December 2016, the ofﬁcial reserve assets increased by
25.6 percent, or by USD 449.1 million (from USD 1 756.81 million
to USD 2 205.93 million) compared with 31 December 2015.

invests
in
safe
instruments
used
by
other
central
banks:
placements on correspondent accounts (usually with other central
banks), term placements in foreign currency and securities, which
are
classiﬁed
into
supranational
securities
(issued
by
supranational institutions), state securities (issued by the U.S.

issuers with high credit rating,
with State guarantee) and

on

(extended by Executive Board Decision no. 168 of 30 December

IBRD became the consultant and the agent of the NBM for
managing a part of the external assets, limited to 20 percent of
ofﬁcial reserve assets.

externally managed assets was of 9.52 percent of the foreign
exchange reserves.

Return on investment depends on market conditions, exchange
rates ﬂuctuations and prices of investment instruments.
The
evolution of the global economic environment and prospects of
development of ﬁnancial markets inﬂuenced the investment
decisions and the composition of foreign exchange reserves. Thus,
the major share in the structure of foreign exchange reserves
structure was held by the USD – 69.7 percent (Chart 3.46).
It should be noted that, de facto, the foreign exchange composition
of reserves may deviate within the range of +/-10 percent of the
normative currency composition (Chart 3.47).
Despite unfavorable conjuncture of global economic environment
and the negative developments of proﬁtability rates of investment
instruments, the decisions taken in the management of foreign
exchange reserves led to a proﬁt of USD 23.67 million in 2016, and
to an average proﬁtability rate of 1.21 percent.

in 2015.
State’s deposits placed with the National Bank of Moldova
In 2016, the Ministry of Finance placed term deposits in MDL with
the National Bank in volume of MDL 56.8 million, with the maturity
from 28 to 303 days.
Compared with 2015, the amount of deposits placed with the
National Bank increased by MDL 25.8 million or about 83 percent.
Placement conditions for deposits remained unchanged throughout
the year, the applied interest rate being equal to the base rate of
the National Bank, valid for the term of the deposit contract.
As at 31 December 2016, the balance of term deposits of the
Ministry of Finance was null.

Annual Report (NBM, 2016)
3.6
Supervision of the bank’s activities
General information
On 31 December 2016, there were 11 banks licensed by the
National Bank of Moldova operating in the Republic of Moldova,
including 4 subsidiaries of foreign banks and ﬁnancial groups.
At the same time, two banks (B.C. “VICTORIABANK” S.A. and BC
“MOLDOVA-AGROINDBANK” S.A.) were under intensive supervision.
Intensive supervision (replacing special supervision,
due to
amendments of the legislation) is maintained as a result of ﬁnding
some indices pointing to the non-transparent structure of
shareholders, bank engagement in high-risk lending operations
and failure to resolve these problems to date.
The National Bank of Moldova prescribed a diagnosis survey to be
carry out by a specialized audit company in the banks concerned.
Following the results of the diagnosis survey, the National Bank
of Moldova adopted decisions on prescribing Remedial Action
Plans to be drafted by BC “MOLDOVA-AGROINDBANK” S.A. and B.C.
“VICTORIABANK” S.A., demanding that all shortcomings identiﬁed
in the survey be resolved.
In August 2016, those two nominated banks submitted the
Remedial
Action
Plans
that
were
examined
and
positively
endorsed by the NBM. For this purpose, the banks took a number
of measures aiming to improve the main activity scope, and
namely: monitoring of shareholders and bank’s related persons,
corporate governance, lending activity, risk management, money
laundering, information technology and etc. The banks report on a
monthly basis to the NBM on the remedial action plans.
The
National Bank monitors the achievement of these plans, notifying,
from time to time, the licensed banks about recommendations
issued
for
their
improvement.
Also,
in
order
to
ensure
transparency of shareholders, the National Bank monitors on an
ongoing basis the compliance by shareholders with the quality
exigencies set out by the legislation of the Republic of Moldova.
Thus, in March 2016, the National Bank of Moldova found that a
group of shareholders of BC “MOLDOVA-AGROINDBANK” S.A. acted
in concert and purchased a substantial share in the share capital of
the bank amounting to 39.58 percent, without prior permission in
writing of the National Bank. Thus, in accordance with Article 15(2)
of the Law on Financial Institutions, the National Bank suspended
the rights on the shares held by the aforementioned shareholders,
notifying that they fall under the provisions of the law concerned
and foreign liability, within 3 months from the date of ﬁnding about
the actions in concert with the violation of the provisions of the
effective law. As a result, the deadline of 3 months expired and
the shareholders failed to alienate the shares. According to the
provisions of the aforementioned law, the executive body of BC
“MOLDOVA-AGROINDBANK” S.A. canceled the shares concerned
and issued new ones, a package of shares that were put up for sale
at the Stock Exchange of Moldova until 26 December 2016. Taking
into account the need to recover corporate governance and attract


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
67
new quality investors, the National Financial Market Commission,
by Decision of 16 December 2016, extended the deadline until
June 2017, in which the bank could put up for sale the newly issued
shares by BC “MOLDOVA-AGROINDBANK” S.A.
As to BC “Moldindconbank” S.A., it is currently being supervised
within early intervention regime applied on 20 October 2016, as
a result of a group of persons acting in concert and purchasing
and holding a substantial share in the share capital of the bank
amounting to 63.89 percent, without prior permission in writing
from the NBM, thus violating the provisions of the Law on Financial
Institution.
Simultaneously, the National Bank suspended the
term of ofﬁce of some members of the management bodies of BC
“Moldindconbank” S.A. and appointed temporary administrators.
At the same time, by the Decision of 20 October 2016, the NBM
ordered assets, debt and own capital evaluations to be carried out
in the bank by an audit company.
It should be noted that, after the application of early intervention
regime, the situation about the liquidity of BC “Moldindconbank”
S.A. remained stable, without signiﬁcant anticipated withdrawals
of deposits.
The total number of subsidiaries of licensed banks from the
Republic of Moldova accounted for 804, out of which 288 branches
and 516 agencies.
Over 2016, 2 branches and 54 agencies were opened and 3
branches and 63 agencies were closed.
As at 31 December 2016, 7 868 people work in the banking sector.
On average, each banking sector employee managed, as at 31
December 2016, assets in value of MDL 9.3 million, up by MDL 0.3
million (3.3 percent) compared with late 2015.
Assets of banks
During 201626, the banking system recorded the following trends:
The total assets of the sector was MDL 72 951.4 million, increasing
by MDL 4 161.2 million (6.0 percent) compared with late 201527.
The increase of assets was driven by both the increase in debt by
MDL 2 919.0 million (5.1 percent), and increase in capital (SIRF)
by MDL 1 242.2 million (10.8 percent).
During the analyzed period, the share of assets in GDP dropped
from 56.1 percent to 54.2 percent (Chart 3.50).

As a result of Moldova’s banking sector concentration analysis,
the Herﬁndahl-Hirschmann indicator (the value of which does not
exceed 10 000) was calculated, based on the bank’s share of
assets in total assets of the banking sector.
This indicator is
calculated by summing the market share squares of all banks
in the sector. The following breakdown is used in international
practice:
• HHI below 100 indicates a market with perfect competition;
• HHI between 100 and 1 500 indicates an unconcentrated
market;
• HHI between 1 500 and 2 500 indicates a moderate
concentration;
• HHI above 2 500 indicates a high concentration.
Thus, following the calculations made, obtaining HHI=1 682.2, it
is concluded that the banking sector of the Republic of Moldova
presents a moderate concentration.

banking sector in terms of its concentration by groups of banks.
The share of Tier I capital of large banks in the Tier I capital per
banking sector accounted for 68.6 percent, increasing by 10.8
percentage points compared with late 2015 due to the migration of
BC “MOBIASBANC˘A-Groupe Societe Generale” S.A. from the group
of medium banks to the group of large banks. Thus, the share
of Tier I capital of medium banks decreased by 11.0 percentage
points and the share of Tier I capital of small banks remained
almost unchanged, amounting to 9.2 percentage points.

and foreign investments.
In order to ensure transparency in the ﬁnancial and banking sector
of the Republic of Moldova, the National Bank, in its capacity
of regulatory and supervisory authority, traces the ownership
structure of banks, checks their shareholders to determine the
correspondence of shareholders with the requirements set out in
the Law on Financial Institutions and the Regulation on holdings in
share capital of the bank, and ensures certainty in identifying the
beneﬁcial owners of holdings by achieving the following:
• permanent follow-up of the transaction with banks’ shares;
• request for submitting information related to the identity
and activity conducted by potential acquirers, including the
direct, indirect shareholders and beneﬁcial owners, as well
as submission of questionnaires, in some cases of other
documents necessary to assess their quality;
• performance of investigations related to the manner of
acquisition of bank’s shares and request for competent
authorities’ assistance;


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
73
• analysis of information/documents, available mass-media
sources
containing
information
about
the
banks’
shareholders.
As a result of the examination of the banks’ ownership structure
and determination of compliance with the requirements of
regulatory acts in force by the banks’ shareholders, the Executive
Board of the National Bank found on 2 March 2016 that a group of
shareholders (20 shareholders) of BC “MOLDOVA-AGROINDBANK”
S.A. acted in concert against the bank, and acquired a qualifying
holding in the share capital of the bank amounting to 39.58
percent, without prior written permission from the National Bank.
At the same time, the Executive Board of the National Bank found
on 20 October 2016 that a group of shareholders (19 shareholders)
of BC “Moldindconbank” S.A. acted in concert against the bank,
acquired and held a qualifying holding in the share capital of the
bank amounting to 63.89 percent, without prior written permission
from the National Bank.
The aforementioned shareholders were informed about the
provisions of Article 15(2) of the Law on Financial Institutions
regarding the suspension of the right to vote, right to convene
and conduct general meetings of shareholders, right to nominate
candidates for the bank’s board and audit committee and right to
receive
dividends.
The
National
Bank
requested
the
aforementioned shareholders to comply with the provisions of the
Law on Financial Institution which sets out the shareholders’
liability to alienate, within 3 months from the date of ﬁnding
concerted activities, the shares related to the qualifying holding
acquired in the share capital of BC “MOLDOVA-AGROINDBANK”
S.A. and BC “Moldindconbank” S.A. without the prior written
permission from the National Bank.
Also, the Executive Board of the National Bank decided on 16 May
2016 to apply sanctions in the form of ﬁne to 5 shareholders of BC
“EuroCreditBank” S.A. for failure to submit information/documents
required for the evaluation of shareholders and to comply with the
exigencies set out by the regulatory acts/laws, which prevents the
NBM from identifying the beneﬁcial owners of qualifying holdings
in the bank.
The National Bank shall further use all instruments provided by
the law to make the situation of shareholding in the banking sector
transparent.
Liabilities of banks
As at 31 December 2016, the liabilities of banks accounted for MDL
60 225.0 million, increasing by MDL 2 919.0 million (5.1 percent)
compared with 31 December 2015. Financial liabilities measured
at amortized cost (customers’ deposits, bonds issued by banks,
other loans, subordinated debts, overnight loans, securities sold
under repo) – held the largest share in total liabilities – MDL 59
464.4 million (98.7 percent), increasing in absolute value by MDL
2 752.4 million (4.9 percent) compared with late 2015. Customers’
deposits accounted for MDL 54 971.0 million or 91.3 percent of
total debts, increasing in absolute value by MDL 4 405.4 million (8.7
percent) compared with 31 December 2015. Also, other liabilities
(overdraft on “Nostro” accounts, overnight loans from the NBM,


74
Annual Report (NBM, 2016)
overnight loans from banks, loans from banks) increased by MDL
198.7 million (51.2 percent), accounting for MDL 586.9 million, tax
debts – by MDL 4.8 million (6.7 percent), accounting for MDL 76.9
million. At the same time, the provisions decreased by MDL 36.9
million (27.6 percent), making up MDL 96.9 million (provisions for
business restructuring, liabilities related to employees’ beneﬁts,
provisions related to damage costs arising from lawsuits against
the bank, funding commitments and other provisions), (Table A.21).
According to prudential reports, the balance of deposits accounted
at 31 December 2016 for MDL 54 838.5 million, increasing by MDL
4 637.0 million (9.2 percent) compared with 31 December 2015, as
a result of the increase of deposits of individuals by MDL 2 651.6
million (7.6 percent), up to MDL 37 669.1 million and deposits of
legal entities – by MDL 1 976.5 million (13.2 percent), up to MDL
16 997.6 million. At the same time, the banks’ deposits decreased
by MDL 8.9 million (5.5 percent), to MDL 171.9 million.
The share of deposits in GDP decreased insigniﬁcantly compared
with the previous year, accounting on the reporting date for 40.8
percent.
As at 31 December 2016, deposits in foreign currency represented
46.5 percent (equivalent of MDL 25 483.8 million) in total deposits,
their absolute value decreased by MDL 853.1 million (3.2 percent)
compared with late 2015, due to the withdrawals of funds – by
MDL 574.5 million, and due to the reassessment resulting from
exchange rate ﬂuctuations – by MDL 275.7 million (deposits
revaluation calculation was made based on EUR, USD, Russian
ruble, Romanian leu and Ukrainian hryvnia, the share of other
currencies was insigniﬁcant). Deposits in MDL accounted for 53.5
percent of total deposits or MDL 29 354.7 million and increased by
MDL 5 490.1 million (23.0 percent) compared with 31 December
2015.

result of migration of BC “MOBIASBANC˘A-Groupe Societe Generale”

Income and expenses of banks
As at 31 December 2016, the proﬁt of the banking sector

by MDL 875.5 million (44.6 percent), as a result of repayment
of granted loans and recorded in the balance of the banks that
are subject to intensive oversight of assets in exchange of loan
repayment.

For the purpose of monitoring banking risks, the National Bank of
Moldova developed stress tests, which are supervising tools used
to assess strong points of each bank and general resistance to
shocks of the banking sector. Stress tests used by the National
Bank focus on credit risks, liquidity risk, interest rate risk and
exchange rate risk and the combinations thereof. All stress tests
30Return on assets=annualized net income/average assets
31Return on equity=annualized net income/average equity
32Net interest margin=annualized net interest/average interest-bearing assets


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
77
are developed based on data reported by licensed banks and are
applied throughout the banking sector, on groups of banks and
individual banks.
Following the stress tests carried out, it was found that the
banking sector is able to withstand most simulated shocks, except
for severe shocks (historical highs) on credit risk affecting
solvency indicators of some individual banks, which drop below
the limits regulated by the National Bank. However, the likelihood
of such scenarios to take shape is reduced and banks take
required measures to improve the quality of loan portfolio, which
allows them to cut losses in case of a stress scenario.
As to liquidity risk, stress test results conﬁrm the high level of
liquidity of the banking sector, banks being able to withstand
deposit withdrawals at maximum historical rates, while liquidity
deﬁcit, for stress test scenarios applied, were not recorded. At the
same time, the National Bank is conducting a study on identifying
the banking sector vulnerability in order to assess the potential
risks that could affect the stability of each bank and of the sector
regarding the capital categories, assets quality, income, liquidity
and sensitivity to market risks. The study results show a low level
of vulnerability of the banking sector as at late 2016, with some
vulnerabilities recorded for sensitivity to market risk, quality of
assets, and proﬁtability.
Banks undergoing windup
We are continuing the windup process of Banca de Economii
S.A., BC “BANCA SOCIAL˘A” S.A. and B.C. “UNIBANK” S.A., which
has been launched in 2015. The process is manifold, including
the recovery process of bank’s assets value conducted by the
liquidators on the territory of the Republic of Moldova and the
investigation conducted by Kroll company abroad, aiming to ﬁnd
and to start recovery of funds that have taken out from the
Republic of Moldova.
The liquidators of banks undergoing windup cooperated with the
criminal prosecution ofﬁcers and legal bodies, took organization
measures to trade assets owned and held by the banks, and
collaborated with borrowers in terms of loans repayment.
Thus, 180 actions (including involving 5 borrowers whose debts
were transferred to one company) had been brought to court until
late 2016 in view of recovering amounts granted previously by
the banks concerned, summing up MDL 14 032 million. At the
same time, 62 criminal cases ﬁled based on liquidators’ claims
(including against 5 borrowers whose debts were transferred to
one company), are managed by the law enforcement agencies.
MDL 698 million (292.5 from Banca de Economii S.A., 282.4 from
BC “BANCA SOCIAL˘A” S.A. and 123.1 from B.C. “UNIBANK” S.A.)
were recovered within the bank’s windup process as at 31
December 2016.
Funds obtained from banks windup are used to pay emergency
loans granted by the National Bank to the banks concerned and
covered by the guarantee of the Government of the Republic of


78
Annual Report (NBM, 2016)
Moldova. Thus, as at 31 December 2016, MDL 861.3 million was
repaid.
Kroll investigation is another aspect of the actions related to the
recovery of funds from these banks.
The ﬁrst steps in the
investigation focused on the identiﬁcation of mechanisms that
triggered the fraud and the development of working partnerships
with international regulatory authorities. Later, the investigation
focused on the prosecution of fraudulent loans granted through
complex money laundering mechanisms to establish their ﬁnal
destination and fraud beneﬁciaries.
The ﬁnal objective of the
investigation is to identify the assets that have been purchased
with fraudulent money and to recover them through legal
procedures. The investigation has progressed and holds sufﬁcient
information to start fund recovery step.
According to the information communicated by Kroll and published
on the NBM’s web page, the documents prove clearly that a
group of at least 75 companies from the Republic of Moldova,
acting in concert, has been involved in the fraud. From 2012 to
2014, signiﬁcant loan volumes have been granted to this group of
companies by those three banks. These funds have predominantly
been transferred on bank accounts opened abroad. Most of these
accounts have been opened on behalf of off-shore companies.
Following this complex money laundering process, most funds
have been redirected to the Republic of Moldova to pay current
loan exposures, to make the impression that they have been true
corporate loans and to allow the extension of the loan portfolio,
while about USD 600 million was dissipated in bank accounts from
various jurisdictions.
This fraud was based on a complex money laundering mechanism
to ensure continuity of the lending process.
The liquidity was
managed and coordinated between the three banks of the Republic
of Moldova through various interbank deposits, with registration of
false guarantees and daily balance sheets submitted to the NBM
and the establishment of a false overdraft in accounting records
to allow continuity of the lending process.
The prosecution of funds is pending and is getting more complex
due to various levels and a network development of money
laundering mechanisms that the fraudulent loan funds were
subject to within the main money laundering mechanism. The
amounts that have been found until now have been prosecuted on
multiple jurisdictions accounts and in other banks of the Republic
of Moldova.
The Kroll team is constantly communicating to the NBM about the
progress of the investigation and has also had high-level meetings
with the anticorruption prosecutor of the Republic of Moldova and
the representatives of some international regulatory and ﬁnancing
agencies.
Kroll, Steptoe and Johnson companies have submitted a strategy
to the NBM to initiate legal proceedings in multiple jurisdictions,
aiming to obtain further information and to recover the assets that
have been misappropriated. Due to the multijurisdictional nature
of proposed legal proceedings and the complexity of processes
involved, it is foreseen that the recovery step will last up to two
years. Besides the assets recovery process, the investigation and
the additional analysis of the fraud will go on to ensure assistance


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
79
in pending legal proceedings and to bring more clarity about the
fraud’s beneﬁciaries. The Kroll company will keep on submitting
reports to the National Bank of Moldova on a regular basis.
3.7
Payment systems
Oversight of payment service providers
In accordance with the Law on payment services and electronic
money no. 114 of 18 May 2012, the oversight of banks as payment
service providers and electronic money issuers is one of the NBM
duties as the supervisory authority.
In this regard, there were conducted 10 on-site investigations in
2016, of which 6 complex and 4 thematic.
During these
investigations,
most non-banking licensed payment service
providers were subject to veriﬁcation. The on-site investigations
allowed for veriﬁcation of aspects related to compliance with the
requirements on protection of funds received from payment
service
users,
transparency
of
conditions
and
information
requirements related to payment services, risk management in
internal control systems and etc.
Following
the
conducted
investigations,
proposals
and
recommendations were made related to actions to be taken
aiming to remove identiﬁed violations and gaps and recovery
measures and penalties were applied, were required.
Also, pursuant to Law on payment services and electronic money
no. 114 of 18 May 2012, the NBM carried out off-site inspections
of the activity performed by payment service providers, including
by constantly monitoring their activity, analyzing quarter reports
submitted to the NBM, examining complaints (claims) related to
payment service providers’ activity and electronic currency issuers,
examining the information published on the websites etc.
Oversight of payment systems
According to Law no. 548-XIII of 21 July 1995 on the National
Bank of Moldova, oversight of payment systems of the Republic of
Moldova is one of the basic tasks of the National Bank of Moldova.
This task is carried out in accordance with the best international
practices, recommendations, principles, standards in this ﬁeld
issued by the Bank for International Settlements, European Central
Bank, International Monetary Fund, World Bank etc., and regulatory
acts in the payment system ﬁeld issued by the NBM.
According to the Policy on payment system oversight in the
Republic of Moldova (approved by the Decision of the Executive
Board of the NBM no.299 of 27 October 2016), the National Bank
of Moldova oversees national payment system facilities, stable
and efﬁcient operation of which is essential for ﬁnancial stability,
monetary policy implementation and promotion of people’s trust
in carrying out cashless payments.
In the ﬁeld of payment and settlement systems, the followings are
subject to oversight:


80
Annual Report (NBM, 2016)
1. Payment and settlement systems (large-value payment
systems and high-volume and low-value payment systems);
2. Securities book-entry systems;
3. Remittances;
4. Payment instruments.
Oversight of payment systems carried out by the National Bank of
Moldova consists of the following steps:
• monitoring and collection of relevant information;
• evaluation of collected information in compliance with the
oversight objectives;
• relevant measures taken according to the law in force if
modiﬁcations are needed.
Automated Interbank Payment Systems
Statistical data
The Automated Interbank Payment System (AIPS) is composed of
the real time gross settlement system (RTGS) and designated-time
net settlement systems (DNS). The RTGS system is designed for
processing large-value and urgent payment, while the DNS system
is designed for processing low-value payments.
In 2016, the payments processed through AIPS declines in number
(by 9.3 percent) and increased in value (by 21.5 percent) compared
with 2015. The number of payments reached MDL 11.9 million,
and their value exceeded MDL 825.8 billion.
In terms of payments structure, the budgetary credit transfers held
the largest share (49.8 percent of the total number of payments),
followed by regular transfers of clients (48.4 percent) and other
types of payments (1.8 percent). In terms of value, the structure of
payments shows a different picture: the value of regular transfers
of clients (18.6 percent of the total value of payments) exceeded
the budgetary credit transfers (14.9 percent), while the transfers
made by banks in the name and on their own account held the
largest share (66.3 percent).
In 2016, the distribution of payments number per AIPS systems
was the following: 8.4 percent for RTGS and 91.6 percent for DNS.
The distribution of payments value shows a reverse picture, so
that 93.9 percent of total payments value was settled through
RTGS and only 6.1 percent – through DNS.

Thus, the total value of payments settled through the RTGS in 2016
