Republic of Moldova, but also shy ﬁnancial ﬂows in the Republic of
Moldova compared with other European states, which is explained
by the inert state of the capital market of the Republic of Moldova.


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
81
In 2016, on average per day33, there were settled through the
RTGS system 3.9 thousand payments in the amount of MDL 3.0
billion, and 43.1 thousand payments in the value of MDL 196.7
million through the DNS system. The average value of a payment
settled in the RTGS system amounted to MDL 774.7 thousand and
that of a payment settled in the DNS system – MDL 4.6 thousand.
Table A.26 presents the evolution of payments settled through the
AIPS compared with 2015.
In late 2016, there were registered 16 participants in AIPS:
• National Bank of Moldova;
• 11 banks licensed by the National Bank of Moldova;
• Settlement Centre from Tiraspol;
• State Treasury of the Ministry of Finance;
• National Securities Depository (NSD) of the Republic of
Moldova;
• Electronic Government Centre, as a mandated participant,
which has not an account in AIPS.
AIPS oversight
The AIPS oversight is carried out to ensure stable and efﬁcient
operation of this system. During 2016, the system’s availability
for its participants was 99.9 percent and was within the limits set
for the system parameters, while major incidents that disrupt the
activity of AIPS and affect its participants did not occur.
State securities book-entry system
The book-entry system of state securities (BES) is a depository and
settlement system for securities issued by the Ministry of Finance
of the Republic of Moldova on behalf of the Government of the
Republic of Moldova and by the National Bank of Moldova, the
ﬁnal settlement of operations carried out in AIPS being achieved
through real time gross settlement system (RTGS), under DvP
principle (delivery versus payment/transfer of ownership). The
BES is organized and managed by the National Bank.
The BES oversight is carried out to ensure stable and efﬁcient
operation of the system. During 2016, the BES availability for its
participants was 100 percent and incidents that disrupt the activity
of the system and participants did not occur.
Cashless payment instruments
Cashless payment instruments are essential components of the
national payment system and the National Bank performs their
oversight in order to ensure efﬁciency and safety of their use.
Payment cards are the most widely used by the population
(individuals) of all payment instruments, the remaining payment
instruments being at the initial stage of use.
It should be
mentioned that the NBM takes signiﬁcant measures agreed with
33The term “day” means operating day.


82
Annual Report (NBM, 2016)
other authorities and international institutions in order to promote
cashless payment instruments and to combat tax evasion.
Payment cards
The number of cards34 in circulation increased by 17.2 percent
and accounted for 1 512 110 units in late 2016 compared with late
2015, reaching the highest level on record.
Amid the increase of number of cards in circulation, the number of
active35 cards had almost the same growth rate (by 16.6
percentage points more than in late 2015), and the share of active
cards of the total number of cards in circulation did not change

signiﬁcantly, making up 60.4 percent. It should be noted that this
rate of card usage is maintained at the same level for the last
recent years.
The share of hybrid payment cards36 (67.9 percent) represents a
majority in the total cards in circulation in the Republic of Moldova.
Contactless cards record a ﬁrm increase, their number being
2.2 times higher than in late 2015 and currently hold a share
of 24.2 percent of total cards in circulation in the Republic of
Moldova, amounting to 366 thousand units. The cards provided
with magnetic stripe only, which in early 2015 held a share of 47.7
percent of total cards in circulation in the Republic of Moldova,
continue their downward trend, dropping to 7.6 percent of total
number of cards. The number of virtual cards had also went down,
representing only 0.4 percent of total cards in circulation in the
Republic of Moldova.
During 2016, 36.29 million operations in number and MDL 42.28
billion in value were carried both domestically and abroad through
payment cards issued by payment service providers of the Republic
of Moldova, increasing by 25.2 percentage points in number and by
19.5 percentage points in value compared with 2015. The number
of cashless payment recorded the highest growth rate, increasing
by 59.4 percent compared with 2015, while the number of cash
withdrawals recorded a shyer growth rate of 9.7 percentage points
compared with 2015. Cash withdrawal is the predominant way
of using payment cards by the card holders of the Republic of
Moldova, making up a share of 60.2 percent of total volume of
operations performed with cards issued in the country. Amid ﬁrm
upward evolution of payment cards for cashless payment purposes,
cash withdrawals will soon become a secondary method of card
usage.
Domestic operations account for 88.4 percent of total transactions
carried out with cards issued in the Republic of Moldova, or
speciﬁcally, they represent 59.7 percent cash withdrawals and
28.7 percent cashless payments made in the country of total
operations performed with cards issued in the Republic of
Moldova.

and legal entities accounted for MDL 349.6 billion during 2016,
increasing by 12.2 percentage points compared with 2015. About
98.7 percent of value of operations through RBSS were carried out
by legal entities.
Other payment instruments
Credit transfer. The payment operations conducted in electronic
format represented 9.2 million in number (63.1 percent of the total
number of credit transfer operations) or MDL 262.6 billion in value
(61.4 percent of the total value of credit transfer operations) of
the total 14.57 million of payment operations in number or MDL
427.82 billion in value made through credit transfer. The rest were
conducted on hard copy (paper).
An upward trend in the use of credit transfers in electronic form
can be notices, amid the decrease of the total number of payment
operations conducted through credit transfer compared with 2015,
which according to estimates, can reach the share of 90 percent
of the total number of credit transfers conducted by bank clients
in a relatively short time.
Direct debit. During 2016, there were conducted 69 871 payment
operations through direct debit, in the total amount of MDL 29.5
million. The total number of users of this instrument was 2 605
persons.

electronic
archiving,
record-keeping
and
management
of
correspondence solution, which were successfully completed.
Also, a number of other strategic projects were initiated and
conducted, aimed to develop the NBM40.

In late 2016, the cash in circulation, in terms of value, amounted to
MDL 18 995.0 million, by 11.4 percent higher than in 2015, when
it recorded the amount of MDL 17 048.0 million.
Banknotes and coins in circulation
Banknotes in circulation, in terms of value, summed up MDL 18
871 million in late 2016, by 11.4 percent more than in late 2015.

Cash in banknotes in circulation, in terms of quantity, according
to the face value structure, accounted for 250 million banknotes
in late 2016, by 8.4 percent more than in 2015. The evolution of
banknotes in monetary circulation, in terms of quantity, is shown
in chart 3.72.
The quantitative increase of banknotes in circulation was due to a
signiﬁcant increase in share of banknotes of MDL 1, MDL 5, MDL
100 and MDL 200 in the total banknotes in circulation.

Commemorative coins and banknotes issuance
Pursuant to Law no. 548 – XIII of 21 July 1995 on the National
Bank of Moldova and the Decision of the Supervisory Board of the
NBM on putting into circulation some commemorative coins as a
means of payment for numismatic purposes, the National Bank
of Moldova issued 7 commemorative coins in 2016, with various
themes, of which 1 gold and 6 silver coins. The volume of coins
was different according to the metal they were minted in, in total
being issued 100 gold coins and 1 500 silver coins.
Table A.27 gives more details on jubilee and commemorative coins
issued in 2016.
3.10
Internal audit
Internal audit (IA) is a stand-alone activity in the NBM aiming to
provide objective advice, to add value and to contribute to the
improvement of the institution’s activities. IA helps the NBM to
accomplish its objectives through a systemic and disciplined
approach within the assessment and improvement of efﬁciency of
processes of risk management, control and governance, providing
to the governing bodies of the institution a reasonable and
objective assurance on their functionality.
In 2016,
Internal Audit Department carried out assurance
missions, the thematic control activities and on-line monitoring of
high-risk areas.
Additionally, and ad-hoc commitment was
undertaken at the request of the NBM management.
Audit
missions and control activities carried out resulted in: 42 reports,
41 advisory/certiﬁcation opinions, with ﬁnal destination toward
the linear managers and members of the NBM governing bodies.
At the same time, IA participated as technical and analytical,
monitoring and counselling advisor within 4 major projects of the
NBM. As a result, IA opinion and suggestions on project scope and
risks were presented either as a result of project documentation
examination, or at the request of the project’s steering committee.
In
order
to
monitor
the
implementation
progress
of
recommendations set forth in audit reports/special reports, IA
carried out quarterly Follow-up audit during the year, which
assessed the status of recommendations submitted both in 2016
and the previous years.
It should be noted that the internal audit activity in 2016 was
unfolded
in
circumstances
of
the
NBM’s
governance


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
89
reconﬁguration. Following legislative amendments to the activity
framework of the NBM, the legislative and the NBM governing
bodies undertook measures in 2016 to strengthen and adjust the
corporate governance. Over 2016, there were created/ﬁlled in all
governing bodies of the NBM, including the Audit Committee of
the NBM was established – a body accountable for internal audit’s
activity monitoring and streamlining.
For 2017, there are activities for the implementation of the Annual
Internal Audit Plan, Program of Assurance and Improvement of
Internal Audit’s Activity Quality, and the Professional Development
Plan of IAD Staff. At the same time, there are also scheduled
programs and activities for the development and elaboration of
the Strategic Internal Audit Plan (medium-term) based on risk
assessment and objectives prioritization.
3.11
Strategic
Plan
of
the
National
Bank of Moldova for 2013-2017
To fulﬁl its role, the functions and activities so that to serve the
best interests of Moldovan citizens, the National Bank of Moldova
has a Strategic Plan that provide the direction of development over
the next ﬁve years. The actual Strategic Plan of the National Bank
of Moldova is approved for 2013-2017.
The Strategic Plan is intended to strengthen the NBM’s role in
society by fulﬁlling all tasks undertaken, ensuring an efﬁcient use
of resources and reducing the exposure to risks. Its priorities set
through strategic objectives are aimed to ensure a high level of
efﬁciency, transparency and performance, by aligning to the best
international practices of communication, credibility and corporate
governance.
The Strategic Plan includes several key elements such as: mission,
vision, values, basic ﬁelds of activity, strategic objectives etc.
Mission of the National Bank of Moldova
The primary objective of the National Bank of Moldova shall be
to ensure and maintain price stability. Without prejudice to the
primary objective, the National Bank of Moldova shall promote and
maintain a ﬁnancial system based on market principles and shall
support the general economic policy of the state.
Vision of the National Bank of Moldova
The National Bank of Moldova is an independent public authority,
efﬁcient and credible, which promotes an adequate monetary
policy and helps ensuring the integrity and stability of the ﬁnancial
system, consistently applying the best international practices in
the interest of society.
Values of the National Bank of Moldova
Civic commitment – activity targeted at the beneﬁt of the public
interest
Efﬁciency – application of innovation and of modern technologies


90
Annual Report (NBM, 2016)
in resources usage;
Excellency – demonstration of competence and professionalism
in exercising and adopting the best international practices;
Transparency
–
transparent
and
impartial
attitude
in
the
decision-making process;
Accountability
–
responsible
attitude
toward
society
in
implementing the assigned tasks;
Integrity – ethical behavior at bank and each employee level;
Credibility
–
conduct
that
inspires
and
maintains
public
conﬁdence.
By establishing the basic ﬁelds of activity the speciﬁc functions of
the NBM are highlighted, these ﬁelds of activity actually reﬂecting
the NBM’s mission and constituting its basic pillars.
The basic ﬁelds of activity are a source of formulation of the
strategic goals, as follow:
• Monetary policy – application of monetary policy instruments
and measures in order to ensure and maintain the price
stability;
• Currency issuance – supporting the economy with national
currency and promoting the national currency’s credibility;
• Supervision
of
ﬁnancial
institutions
–
regulation
and
supervision of ﬁnancial institutions’ activity;
• Payment
system
–
promotion
of
efﬁciency,
safety,
accessibility and innovative nature of payments.
The strategic objectives are the NBM priorities set based on its
mission, for a deﬁnite period of time, in order to help achieve its
vision.
For the fourth consecutive year, since the Strategic Plan has been
implemented, the NBM has continued to further strive to achieve
its strategic objectives. The most important achievements in 2016
related to the NBM strategic objectives are as follow:


 
Strategic 
objectives 
Major achievements in 2016 
1. Ensure price 
stability 
In order to support the monetary policy decisions and to develop the capacities of 
macroeconomic analysis and forecast of the national economy behaviour and inflationary 
process, a number of forecasts and researches have been made during 2016, such as: there 
was drawn up the forecasting model of the monetary aggregate as satellite to the central 
forecasting model, the projection of main indicators of the balance of payments; there was 
developed the model for estimating the currency and the deposits of other sectors by 
analysing and highlighting the cash and currency transactions against Moldovan leu.  
2. Increase the 
credibility of the 
national currency 
There were organized and carried out workshops for the cashiers of the NBM and licensed 
banks, which covered aspects related to the maintenance of the qualitative cash into 
circulation, as the combat of the forgery trend of the monetary marks in order to decrease 
the forgery and counterfeit risks of the national currency.  
3. Develop the 
supervision 
function of the 
NBM  
The NBM Strategy for the implementation of Basel III standards through the European 
legislative framework was approved. 
Draft Law on banks' activity and investment firms and its new regulation framework were 
drawn up.  
The impact of the Basel III implementation on banking sector of the Republic of Moldova was 
assessed.  
Draft Law on additional supervision of the credit institutions, of the insurers/reinsurers and 
investment societies that are part of a financial conglomerate.  
A national legal framework on banking shareholding transparency and quality, corporate 
governance and risks administration, including the aspects related to external audit conduct 
has been strengthened through the amendment of the Law on financial institutions.  
The IT solution of Credit Risk Register was put into operation, which aims to provide sufficient 
operational information to the National Bank of Moldova and will contribute to: optimize the 
prudential regulation and strengthen the banking supervision within a macro prudential 
framework of banks’ lending supervision, in accordance with modern principles of credit risk 
management, to evaluate the banks’ ability to overcome financial stress events, and help 
tracking bank solvency and macroeconomic stability. 
4. Promote 
cashless 
payments and 
reduce cash in 
circulation 
 
National bank contributed to the financial education of the civil society in the cashless 
payment field by modernizing its webpage www.faranumerar.md and publishing more articles 
in this area. At the same time, there was initiated a feasibility study on immediate payments 
implementation within national payment system. 
 
 
 
 
Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
91


5. Strengthen the 
financial stability 
function 
National Bank contributed to the developments of the legal framework on single Central 
Securities Depository creation and initiated this project that aims at implementing the legal 
provisions approved.  
Law on banks' recovery and resolution and its legal framework amendments was drawn up, 
both drafts were approved in the same time by the Parliament of the Republic of Moldova. 
6. Improve 
external 
communication 
A consistent policy of informing the public on matters related to the NBM duties has been 
supported and promoted, in particular the systematic and timely information concerning the 
monetary policy decisions and actions involving financial stability insurance. 
The NBM has hosted the "International Financial Education Week" (Global Money Week), 
carried out under the slogan "Take Part. Save Smart". During this event, NBM called upon 
children and youth, inviting them to participate to various activities oriented towards public 
awareness of the knowledge in economic and financial field at consumer level.   
A leaflet for children "A travel in the world of money", available electronically on the NBM 
official webpage, has been edited in partnership with European Fund for South East Europe 
(EFSF). 
There were held conferences for media representatives, meeting with pupils and students on 
subjects of interest for participants such as: development of the abilities to manage money 
correctly, the money history and function etc.  
There were organized seminars for media representatives, Ministry of Finance, Ministry of 
Economy and National Bureau of Statistics of the Republic of Moldova with the topic: 
"Methodological changes in balance of payments in the context of new international 
standards", "External debt statistics", "Dissemination of statistical data on external sector on 
the NBM official webpage (orientation)". 
There were organized two concerts for the general public, with jazz and classic music on the 
occasion of the 25th anniversary of the National Bank of Moldova and Independence Day of 
the Republic of Moldova.  
NBM organised the Doors Open Day, giving the public the opportunity to visit the exhibition 
hall of the bank and get more information about the banknotes and coins in circulation since 
the establishment of the NBM until now.  
7. Improve the 
NBM operational 
activities 
efficiency 
The implementation of a single trading platform for the monetary foreign exchange 
instruments through Bloomberg information system has been completed.  
The NBM activity processes have been optimized through the implementation of certain 
medium-term opportunities, having as impact the increase in efficiency of the use of bank's 
internal resources through: the automation and reduction of processes' duration, the 
decision-making acceleration and documents' circuit optimization also through the electronic 
signature implementation. 
Law on settlement finality in payment and financial instruments settlement systems; Law on 
financial collateral arrangements; Law on amendment and completion of legislative acts in 
92
Annual Report (BNM, 2016)


 
accordance with CPSS-IOSCO Recommendations have been approved. 
IT technology function has been developed by: modernising the network and servers 
infrastructure, migration to MS Office 2016 services, Outlook, Skype for Business, IP 
telephony; implementation of a modern instrument for IT technologies services management.  
The solution for the correspondence electronic archiving, recording and management has 
been implemented.  
8. Streamline the 
human resources 
management 
 
There were taken decisions to streamline the bank's organisational structure, being ensured 
the segregation of certain important functions such as: banking supervision, banking 
resolution, financial stability and combat of money laundering and terrorist financing. The 
creation of some independent subdivisions that will grant support to the Executive Board in 
carrying out the listed functions will enhance the NBM institutional capacity and its adapting 
to new steadily growing requirements of the internal and external environment.  
The first implementation phase of a new wage system for the NBM employees has been 
completed, by achieving the following benefits: raise of the system transparency level; raise 
the awareness of the individual wage level; increase the NBM activity as an employer. The 
second phase that supposes to establish a direct relationship between the position value and 
salary level, as well as ensuring an internal equity and an external salary competitiveness will 
be completed in 2017.  
The recruiting process was developed by extending the area of announcements publication 
using largely specialized social networks, recruiting activity intensification, implementation of 
an online registration instrument.   
9. Develop the 
corporate 
governance 
 
Several actions have been taken during the year, designed to develop the team spirit and 
increase the employee retention capacity. 
There were conducted internal communications on achieving the bank's strategic plan, the 
conduct of major projects in order to share the activity results by all bank's employees and to 
increase the changes absorption capacity, while the most significant changes were 
communicated in details by each employee.   
At the same time, there were organised charity and cultural events, as well for environment 
improvement, sportive championships in order to support the social responsibility of the 
bank's employees. There were also organized some events for the employees' children to 
better know the bank's activity, exhibition room and to enhance their financial knowledge.  
An internal survey was conducted to collect the employees' opinions and proposals on various 
aspects such as: relationship with the heads of subdivisions, professional development 
opportunities, the working environment and conditions, internal subdivision culture in order 
to ensure a bilateral communication channel between the management and employees. As a 
result of the survey, the corresponding level of the NBM employees activity (satisfied level) 
reached 70 percent in 2016.

3.12
International Cooperation of the
Republic of Moldova
International Monetary Fund (IMF)
The International Monetary Fund’s basic mission is to ensure
international monetary system’s stability. In order to achieve this
mission, the International Monetary Fund targets its activity at the
monitoring of economic and ﬁnancial policies of member states,
lending of member states who face payment balance disruption
and granting technical assistance to the IMF member countries.
The Republic of Moldova is a member of the IMF since 12 August
1992.
The 14th General Review of Quotas and Reform of the Executive
Board initiated by the IMF entered into force in 2016. Although
these reforms were voted by the IMF member states as early as
December 2010, they entered into force only at the beginning of
2016, after being accepted by USA, playing a decisive role within
the IMF, in December 2015. These reforms set out the double
increase of the IMF capital, adjustment of member states quotas
to reﬂect more accurately their position in the world economy and
the change of the way the Executive Board of the IMF is being
constituted.
In this context, the subscription quota of the Republic of Moldova
to the IMF increased by SDRs 49.3 million, accounting, in late
reference year, for SDRs 172.5 million (about USD 231.2 million) or
0.04 percent of the IMF capital. The voting power of the Republic
of Moldova in the IMF is of 1973 votes, representing 0.08 percent
of the total number of votes.
Over 2016, the National Bank of Moldova’s relations with the
IMF unfolded amid the visits of post-program monitoring and
negotiations of a new economic program supported by the IMF.
In this sense, the IMF experts undertook 3 working visits to
Chis, in˘au in 2016. The ﬁrst two visits that took place from 23 to 29
February and from 23 to 27 May, focused on the analysis of
economic evolution and discussions of policies promoted by the
authorities in order to maintain macroeconomic stability and to
improve the governing in the ﬁnancial sector.
The third working visit of the IMF mission to the Republic of
Moldova occurred from 5 to 15 July. The mission aimed to start
discussions on a potential program supported by the IMF and to
reach an agreement on a set of policies that would contribute to
the maintenance of macroeconomic stability, improvement of
corporate
governance
and
increase
of
transparency
of
shareholders in the banking sector, as well as stimulation of
sustainable and comprehensive economic growth.
Subsequently, the authorities of the Republic of Moldova agreed
with the IMF on a complex program of economic and ﬁnancial
reforms to be implemented by our country.
As a result, on 7
November 2016, the Executive Board of the IMF adopted a
decision to grant ﬁnancial assistance to the Republic of Moldova


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
95
through two lending facilities – Extended Fund Facility (EFF) and
the Extended Credit Facility (ECF), for three years, aiming to
support the state’s economic and ﬁnancial reforms program.
The main objective of the program supported by the IMF was to
solve emergency problems related to the quality of governance
and stability in the banking sector. Hence, the program focuses
on successful rehabilitation of banks of systemic importance and
radical improvement of the regulation and supervision framework
of banks, as well as the banks’ crisis management framework,
including through a basic change of the implementation mode and
penalties.
In this context, the Republic of Moldova obtained access to a total
amount of SDRs 129.4 million (about USD 178.7 million). The ﬁrst
installment, amounting to SDRs 26 million (about USD 35.9 million),
was made available to the authorities of the Republic of Moldova
in November 2016. The remaining amount shall be disbursed
during the implementation of the program, in ﬁve installments, the
disbursement being conditioned by program assessments carried
out during the IMF missions.
The previous IMF assistance program for the Republic of Moldova
was
completed
in
April
2013
and
combined
two
funding
mechanisms: in concessional terms (Extended Crediting Facility
(ECF)) and in non-concessional terms (Extended Fund Facility
(EFF)). In this context, the Republic of Moldova received ﬁnancial
assistance in the amount of SDRs 320.0 million, of which SDRs
225.0 million were received by the National Bank of Moldova and
SDRs 95.0 million – by the Ministry of Finance.
During 2016, the National Bank of Moldova received from the IMF
the amount of SDRs 8.5 million (USD 11.7 million) granted through
the EFF funding facility, to strengthen international reserves of
the Republic of Moldova. Table A.28 shows the cumulative funding
arrangements that the National Bank of Moldova received until the
end of 2016.
In 2016, the total payments related to the servicing of loans
granted by the IMF to the National Bank of Moldova constituted
SDRs 36.44 million (equivalent to USD 50.59 million), of which the
payments for the principal amount of the loan constituted SDRs
35.04 million (equivalent to USD 48.63 million), and payments for
the interest rates on loans – SDRs 1.40 million (equivalent to USD
1.96 million).
World Bank
The World Bank Group reunites several international institutions:
International
Bank
for
Reconstruction
and
Development,
International Development Association, International Finance
Corporation, Multilateral Investment Guarantee Agency and the
International Centre for Settlement of Investment Disputes. All
these organizations collaborate tightly to achieve 2 fundamental
objectives:
elimination of poverty and promotion of shared
prosperity. The Republic of Moldova became a member of the
World Bank in 1992 and over the years received valuable ﬁnancial
and technical assistance for various development projects. The
Last Partnership Strategy of the World Bank Group for the
Republic of Moldova covers the period 2014-2017 and is based on
3 pillars: 1) growth of competition; 2) improvement of human


96
Annual Report (BNM, 2016)
capital and minimization of social risks; 3) promotion of a green,
clean, and environmentally resistant Moldova. Improvement of
competition in the Republic of Moldova with the support of the
World Bank, would contribute to the development of the private
sector and the growth in number of jobs, due to the improvement
of the business environment, settlement of issues related to the
transparency, accountability and governance, and improvement
of access to funding. In this context, the Partnership Strategy of
the World Bank Group for the Republic of Moldova encourages
continuous development of the ﬁnancial sector, including by
reducing the lending risk from banks, facilitating the creation of
some modern and efﬁcient payment services, strengthening the
protection of ownership of shareholders by improving the
corporate governance and shareholders’ transparency, as well as
building supervision capacity of the National Bank of Moldova. All
these issues are always on the agenda of discussions between the
representatives of the National Bank of Moldova and those of the
World Bank Group, being approached in 2016 too during countless
meetings, including during joint reunions of the Council of
Governors of the World Bank Group and the Council of Governors
of the International Monetary Fund.
European Bank for Reconstruction and Development
The objective of the European Bank for Reconstruction and
Development (EBRD) is to sustain the economic development of
countries from central and Eastern Europe, south and eastern
Mediterranean Sea and Central Asia, as well as the extension of
market
economy
principles,
promotion
of
private
and
entrepreneurship initiative and application of pluralist democracy
principles. The Republic of Moldova became member of the EBRD
in 1992. The recent country strategy for the Republic of Moldova,
approved by the EBRD Board on 30 April 2014 sets out several
strategic
priorities,
among
which
the
creation
of
required
conditions to support the development of the private sector. In
order to achieve this priority, the EBRD pays close attention to the
improvement of the process of corporate governance and
transparency in the banking sector, thus maintaining an active
cooperation with relevant authorities from the Republic of
Moldova.
In the EBRD Board, the management body of this
organization, the function of governor on behalf of the Republic of
Moldova is held by the representative of the Ministry of Economy,
and the function of alternate governor – by the representative of
the National Bank of Moldova.
In 2016, the National Bank of
Moldova continued the dialogue with the EBRD, including by
participating in the annual reunion of the Council of EBRD
Governors, where issues related to the banking sector and the
relationship with the International Monetary Fund were discussed,
among others.
European Union (EU)
The
legal
framework
of
conducting
collaboration
relations
between our country and the European Union is represented by
the Association Agreement between the Republic of Moldova, on
one side, and the European Union and the European Atomic
Energy Community and its member states, on the other side, that
was signed in Brussels, on 27 June 2014 and applied on a
provisional basis as at 1 September 2014.
The Association Agreement between the Republic of Moldova and
the European Union entered into force on 1 July 2016, meaning


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
97
the end of its ratiﬁcation procedure by the signatory parties and
full application of all provisions.
In order to monitor the European integration progress at national
level, the Government of the Republic of Moldova approved,
during the meeting of 28 December 2016, a National Action Plan
on the Implementation of the Association Agreement Republic of
Moldova – European Union (NAPAA) for 2017-2019. This document
replaces the Action Plan on the Implementation of the Association
Agreement Republic of Moldova – European Union for 2014-2016
and was developed based on proposals made by concerned public
institutions,
including
the
NBM,
covering
key
priorities
of
cooperation in order to achieve the commitments set forth in the
Association Agreement with the European Union.
The National Bank of Moldova committed to contribute to the
implementation of this Plan undertaking a range of actions, as
follow: alignment of the legislation on the central bank to the best
EU practices, consolidation of the NBM capacity in the ﬁeld of
banking
regulation
and
supervision,
development
and
strengthening of requirements related to governance and banking
risk management,
interpretation and implementation of EU
legislation in the ﬁeld of ﬁnancial and banking services etc.
The Governmental Commission for European Integration, the

meetings
of
the
Governmental
Commission
for
European

the
progress
in
the
RM-EU
Association
Agenda,
the
implementation of the DCFTA, the priorities for 2016.
Amid the collaboration between the Republic of Moldova and the

cooperation
and
other
sectors
(cluster
I:
23
June
2016),

During
these reunions, there were discussed a series of topics concerning

strengthening the banking sector, as well as in the ﬁeld of banking
regulation and supervision, the progress related to banking sector
reforms, measures to prevent fraud in the banking sector, the
progress in implementing the Association Agreement and etc.
Over 2016, the National Bank of Moldova cooperated with other
central banks from the European Union aiming to take over
experience in various ﬁelds that refer to its range of competence.
Thus, the NBM representatives conducted working visits to the
central banks of Romania, Poland and Germany.
Also in 2016, the National Bank of Moldova continued to receive
assistance from the European Union through Twinning program,
aimed to build the central bank’s capacity in the ﬁeld of banking

regulation
and

amid

Group of Banking Supervisors from Central and Eastern

In 2016, the National Bank of Moldova, as the member of the

Thus, following the request of the central bank of
