Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
99
External
technical
assistance
and
cooperation
with
international
institutions
and
central
banks
of
other
states
It should be mentioned that amid the cooperation of the National
Bank of Moldova with international bodies, the IMF experts
conducted from 14 to 22 November 2016 a mission of evaluation
of credibility and safeguard of the National Bank of Moldova
(Safeguard Assessments 2016). This evaluation is a fundamental
element in the Republic of Moldova’s attempt to seek funding
within extended funding mechanisms – ECF and EFF, granted by
the IMF. During the working visit, the IMF team met with senior
management representatives and linear managers, with the
purpose to evaluate the level of credibility and safeguard of the
National Bank of Moldova through ELRIC (mechanism of external
audit,
legal
framework
and
autonomy,
ﬁnancial
reports,
mechanisms of internal audit, internal control system) pillars. The
IMF team of experts analyzed also the measures taken by the
National Bank of Moldova to implement the recommendations
made at the previous evaluation conducted in 2010.
The National Bank of Moldova received technical assistance from
more external donors in 2016 aiming to strengthen the banking
regulation and supervision capacity.
Thus, in the context of the Program of the European Union High
Level Policy Advice Mission to the Republic of Moldova, a high level
advisor from the European Union has been providing assistance to
the National Bank of Moldova since 15 February 2016 aiming to
improve the banking supervision and banking resolution practices.
In this sense, the National Bank of Moldova’s representatives
received assistance for the development of new monthly analysis
models of the ﬁnancial situation of licensed banks, elaboration of
press releases on the ﬁnancial situation of the banking sector, and
the modiﬁcation of internal procedures on the off-site and on-site
supervision.
During 2016, the National Bank of Moldova continued to receive
support from the experts of the National Bank of Romania and the
central bank of the Netherland within the Twinning project funded
by the European Union, which started on 30 June 2015. A range of
activities were conducted under the form of missions or working
visits for the transposition of the European CRD IV framework
(Directive 2013/36/EU and the Regulation no. 575/2013), during
which the way of implementing the European provisions in the
national legislation of the Republic of Moldova was discussed. In
this regard, the following progress was recorded:
• the draft of the new banking law was elaborated and
subjected to public consultation, transposing the European
framework
–
Law
on
banks’
activity
and
investment
companies;
• the secondary regulations elaboration process was completed,
aimed to enforce the new banking law provisions, some of
them being presented to the banking sector representatives;
• the impact evaluation on EU legislation provisions on the
banking sector of the Republic of Moldova was completed
and meetings were conducted with the representatives of the
licensed banks to discuss the outcomes of such activities.


100
Annual Report (BNM, 2016)
The actions conducted during 2016 within the Twinning project
aimed to strengthen the supervision function of the National Bank
of Moldova and to establish adequate prudential requirements
for risks existing in the ﬁnancial and banking system. Also, the
transposition of the European regulations in the ﬁeld will allow
the legislative framework to be qualiﬁed as being equivalent to
the European Union one, and, implicitly, to establish favorable
conditions to attract investors from European countries in the
Republic of Moldova and to develop the activities of the domestic
banks through their access on the banking markets of other states.
The National Bank of Moldova still orients its efforts toward
achieving the goal of maintaining ﬁnancial stability of the banking
system in the Republic of Moldova.
Thus, the National Bank
received during 2016 technical assistance from the U.S. Treasury
on ensuring the continuity of the activities related to banking
supervision, including on improving banking resolution practices
and on developing a guide relating to contingency plans.
In 2016,
the National Bank of Moldova received technical
assistance from experts of PricewaterhouseCoopers through the
“Good Governance Fund” funded by the Government of Great
Britain aimed to monitor the licensed banks’ shareholding, to
elaborate an analysis report related to the banking sector
contagion, to draft speciﬁcations related to money laundering and
terrorism ﬁnancing solutions etc.
One of the major concerns of the National Bank of Moldova is
the improvement of analysis capacity in the ﬁeld of shareholders’
transparency. In this context, from 11 to 13 January 2016, the
National Bank of Moldova’s representatives received technical
assistance from the central bank of the Netherlands, under the
form of a study visit.
The purpose of the visit was to study
processes and the solution that assists the banking supervisors
from the central bank of the Netherlands in managing information
and relationships with supervised entities.
Also, in the context of bilateral cooperation for the banking
regulation and supervision, the National Bank of Moldova’s
representatives received technical assistance from the central
bank of Germany, in the form of a study visit conducted from 12
to 14 July 2016. The visit focused on familiarizing with German
practices
related
to
shareholders’
transparency
monitoring
process; principles, legal framework and practical application of
internal evaluation process of capital adequacy and supervision
and assessment process.
In 2016, the National Bank of Moldova, amid banking licensing
function, collaborated with banking supervision authorities from
other
states
(Italy,
Kazakhstan,
Lithuania,
Bosnia
and
Herzegovina,
Serbia),
accomplishing a mutual exchange of
information required for the evaluation process of people quality
(“Fit&Proper”)
who
exercised
an
activity
authorized
and
supervised by these authorities.
In order to improve analytical capacity, the National Bank of
Moldova received advice in 2016 on ﬁscal issues relevant to the
monetary policy formulation process. The assistance was provided
by the European Bank for Reconstruction and Development.
An economic research framework allowing to track the latest
evolutions in the economy plays a leading role in the formulation


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
101
of monetary policy in a central bank.
Thus, in the context of
bilateral cooperation with the central bank of Poland, the NBM
representatives paid a study visit to the central bank of Poland on
12 April 2016.
The technical assistance focused on the
familiarization with the experience of the central bank of Poland in
terms
of
implementing
the
economic
research
framework;
developing the Strategy on economic research; collaborating with
internal and external experts in relation with the research projects
and research advice, as part of the economic research program
and with international research networks.
One of the main duties of the National Bank of Moldova is to
maintain and manage state’s foreign exchange reserves. Over
2016, the National Bank of Moldova continued participating in the
RAMP (Reserves Advisory and Management Program) program,
provided by the World Bank. This assistance contributes to the
improvement of state’s foreign exchange reserves management
quality, through experience, information and practice exchange
between the program’s member states. Also, there are seminars
within the program that are organized from time to time, during
which innovations, techniques and methods of management of
foreign exchange reserves are being presented, as well as methods
of estimate and investment risk mitigation associated with this
process.
In the context of the NBM actions regarding the creation of the
single Central Securities Depositary (CSD) to increase the safety
and efﬁciency of the clearing and settlement system for the
securities of the Republic of Moldova, the NBM received two
technical assistance missions from the International Monetary Fund
in 2016. The ﬁrst mission took place from 26 January to 5 February
2016 and focused on the establishment of the CSD, formulation
of some recommendations related to the CSD governance and
functionality, and its legal and supervision form.
The second
mission took place from 26 to 29 April 2016 and aimed to establish
the need to carry out legislative amendments for the establishment
of the CSD.
One of the National Bank of Moldova’s basic activity is the
promotion of efﬁciency, safety, accessibility and innovation
character of payments.
In this sense, the National Bank of
Moldova received technical assistance, in bilateral format, from
the central bank of Poland. Thus, from 26 to 28 April 2016, the
NBM representatives made a working visit to the central bank of
Poland during which there were shared experiences on the
development of the national payment system through the
implementation
of
some
innovative
payments
(immediate
payments), promotion strategies of cashless payments, and
supervision of payment systems carried out by the central bank.
In order to ensure stability of the national currency circulation, the
National Bank of Moldova’s goal is to promote a policy that supplies
the economy with national currency in accordance with the real
needs of currency circulation and to maintain its quality while in
circulation, following the continuity and credibility principles. Thus,
the NBM received technical assistance from the central banks
of Georgia and Romania in 2016, aimed to familiarize with and
take over the experience of other central banks in the ﬁeld of
cash management (management, storage, processing, supply of
customers with cash).


102
Annual Report (BNM, 2016)
Amid the activities of the National Bank of Moldova in the ﬁeld of
operations
with
cash,
a
major
topic
is
the
ﬁght
against
counterfeiting of currency,
especially the cooperation with
relevant institutions. It is also worth mentioning the need to carry
out some amendments to the national legislation in this ﬁeld,
especially concerning the alignment of the national legislation to
the European Union one. In this sense, the NBM representatives
made a working visit in late March 2016 to the central bank of
Poland, whose experience in ﬁghting against money counterfeit is
very important for the National Bank of Moldova.
Also, from 29 March to 12 April 2016, the National Bank of Moldova
received assistance from the IMF in the ﬁeld of cash management
from the viewpoint of combatting money counterfeiting.
The
technical assistance was provided by an expert in cash operations
from the central bank of Belgium.
In order to improve the capacity and efﬁciency in fulﬁlling the
function of national supplier of statistics, the NBM representatives
made two study visits to the central bank of Germany. The ﬁrst
visit occurred from 27 to 29 September 2016 and approached
the topic “Securities statistics according to the methodology of
the European Central Bank”. The second visit, which occurred
from 22 to 23 November 2016, approached the topic “Statistics”
and focused on topics concerning the elaboration of statistics on
international accounts according to the recommendations of the
IMF, of statistical indicators from the Agreed Investigation relating
to direct foreign investments and country’s ﬁnancial accounts.
The National Bank of Moldova represents the Republic of Moldova
within the Technical Working Group on Trade in Services Statistics,
within the Central European Free Trade Agreement (CEFTA), to
which the Republic of Moldova is a member of since 2007. The
CEFTA
aims
to
adapt
member
states’
economies
to
the
community market lacking customs duties and to conduct and
exercise of pre-adhesion to the European Union. The main reason
of participation in the working group was to support negotiations
on trade in services and to analyze the impact of free trade, as
well as to create a statistical data reporting system fully functional
for the CEFTA Secretariat. The technical working group on trade in
services statistics has recently taken over topics related to
domestic statistics of foreign subsidiaries and direct foreign
investments.
In 2016,
the CEFTA Secretariat organized a
statistically methodological meeting and carried out pre-work for
the establishment of a reporting system designed for member
countries. The National Bank of Moldova prepared the ﬁrst set of
data.
The development of information technologies plays a signiﬁcant
role in increasing operational efﬁciency in a central bank,
dictating the way in which the central bank carries out its
activities,
by automatic more activity processes and using
information systems with high-end architecture. In this context,
the NBM representatives made a study visit to the central bank of
Germany from 15 to 17 November 2016. The representatives of
other central banks from Central and South-East Europe also
participated in the visit. The visit aimed to ensure experience
exchange on the IT organization in a central bank.
Amid the improvement of project management capacity and
following
the
establishment
of
a
programs
and
projects


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
103
management function, the National Bank of Moldova received
technical assistance from the central bank of Germany on the
topic “Project management and control” from 28 to 30 June 2016.
The shared experience contributed to streamlining the project
portfolio management process initiated by the National Bank of
Moldova.
The availability of a modern human resources management system
plays an important role in making the organization’s business
more efﬁcient. In this sense, it should be noted that in 2016 the
National Bank of Moldova received technical assistance through
the program funded by the Government of Great Britain – “Good
Governance Fund”, on topics relating to the remuneration system
evaluation of the National Bank of Moldova compared with the
banking system, general market and specialized services market.
This evaluation served as support for further launching of a project
of improving the remuneration system of the NBM employees by
job assessment, competences and performance.
Also, amid the need to strengthen the human resources for the
purpose of banking regulation and supervision, the National Bank
of Moldova received technical assistance from the National Bank
of Romania within the Twinning project on topics relating to
performance
evaluation,
staff
recruitment,
professional
development etc.
Consumers’ ﬁnancial awareness and promotion of ﬁnancial
education among the population, among other factors, inﬂuence
the creation of good conditions for a sustainable economic growth
and ﬁnancial stability.
The National Bank of Moldova is focused on participating in the
promotion
of
ﬁnancial
education,
encouraging
also
the
contribution of other institutions on this sense. According to the
Road Map Matrix on the improvement of competition (approved by
Government Decision no.4 of 14 January 2014 and amended by
Government Decision no.806 of 29 June 2016), the National Bank
of Moldova, jointly with other relevant authorities, committed to
develop the National Strategy on Financial Education. It should be
noted that the National Bank of Moldova assumed the role of the
leader in the development process of this strategy. Thus, in the
context of advisory agreement signed by the National Bank of
Moldova, jointly with the Development Facility of the European
Fund
for
Southeast
Europe
(EFSE
DF)
and
the
regional
Microﬁnance Center sp. Z o.o. (MFC) of Poland, the National Bank
of
Moldova
received
support
from
MFC
experts
for
the
development of the National Strategy on Financial Education from
September 2015 to February 2016.
In 2016, amid ﬁnancial education promotion, the National Bank of
Moldova
received
assistance
from
the
European
Fund
for
Southeast Europe for the organization of activities conducted
under the events: “International Financial Education Week” and
“International Savings Day”.
In particular,
for an efﬁcient
interaction with target-groups from secondary schools and a
better
conduct
of
such
events,
an
interactive
game
was
developed with basic terms from the banking ﬁeld, by a certain
age category. This approach boosted the attractiveness of events
organized by the National Bank of Moldova and contributed to the
expansion of the indirect effect of ﬁnancial education.


104
Annual Report (BNM, 2016)
The interactive games practice started in 2016 aimed to make the
public aware of the importance of ﬁnancial decisions at consumer
level and to optimize the actions with ﬁnancial inclusion impact.
3.13
Corporate
governance
of
the
National Bank
Corporate governance of the National Bank is ensured by the
Supervisory Board, in charge for the organization of an efﬁcient
system of free public supervision of the National Bank’s activity
and the Executive Board, which is in charge for the executive
management of the National Bank and ensures the independent
fulﬁllment of the bank’s basic duties.
A range of actions targeted at the adjustment of the legal and
regulatory framework were carried out during 2016, as well as
organization processed which contributed to the strengthening of
the NBM’s governance framework.
The following persons were appointed in ofﬁce:
• the governor of the NBM,
• 4 members of the Supervisory Board, who are not employees
of the NBM,
• First Deputy Governor and Deputy Governor of the NBM.
Following these nominations, the NBM’s Supervisory Board and
the Executive Board seats were ﬁlled in numerically pursuant to
Article 23 of the Law on the National Bank of Moldova.
Activity of the Supervisory Board
In order to fulﬁll its duties, the Supervisory Board convened 10
meetings in 2016, including in full membership (4 members from
5 October 2016), during which 26 decisions were approved (17 –
in full membership) on the following topics:
• Approval of the annual report and the ﬁnancial situation of
the NBM for 2015.
• Establishment
of
the
Audit
Committee,
consisting
of
3members who are not employees of the National Bank.
• Nomination of the general auditor and the establishment of
remuneration conditions.
• Approval of the cost estimate, salary pool and investment
beneﬁts of the National Bank.
• Establishment of lending conditions for the NBM employees.
• Approval of graphic designs of commemorative coins.
• Approval of the monthly allowance amount for the members
of the Supervisory Board who are not members of the
Executive Board.


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
105
• Approval of internal regulations.
Through its activity, the Supervisory Board paid high attention
to the examination of international experience in the ﬁeld and of
the best practices, thanks to its permanent collaboration with the
representatives of the development partners of the Republic of
Moldova and other ﬁnancial and banking institutions on topics of
mutual interest.
The Supervisory Board promoted decision-making transparency
and carried out the monitoring of the NBM activities related to its
legal duties.
Over the year, 2 decisions of the Supervisory Board were submitted
for publication in the Ofﬁcial Monitor of the Republic of Moldova
(according to Table A.35 from the attachments).
Activity of the Executive Board
In order to fulﬁll its duties, the Executive Board convened 74
meetings during 2016, during which 376 decisions were taken on
the following topics:
• Monetary and Foreign Exchange Policy of the National Bank
of Moldova,
• Banking regulation and supervision,
• Oversight Policy of Payment Systems of the Republic of
Moldova,
• Accounting Policy of the National Bank of Moldova,
• Balance of payments,
• Approval of internal regulations.
The Executive Board continued to promote the application of
monetary policy instruments and measures in order to ensure and
maintain price stability; to supply the economy with national
currency and to ensure national currency credibility; to regulate
and supervise activities of ﬁnancial institutions, and to ensure
efﬁciency, security, accessibility and innovation character of
payments.
Over the year, there were 44 decisions of the Executive Board
that had been published in the Ofﬁcial Monitor of the Republic of
Moldova (according to Tables A.35 and A.36 in the attachments).
Activity of Audit Committee
Audit Committee is established pursuant to the Decision of the
Supervisory Board of the National Bank of Moldova of 5 October
2016 and carries out its activity according to Articles 26 and 28
of the Law on the National Bank of Moldova and the Regulation
on the operation of the Audit Committee of the National Bank of
Moldova, approved by the Decision of the Supervisory Board of
the National Bank of Moldova of 22 December 2016.
Pursuant to Article 28 of the Law on the National Bank of Moldova,
the Audit Committee has the following main duties:


106
Annual Report (BNM, 2016)
• to monitor the ﬁnancial reporting process of the National
Bank,
• to monitor the efﬁciency of the internal control system and
risk management,
• to monitor and to manage the internal audit function,
• to monitor the independence and the activity of the external
audit.
Since the establishment of the Audit Committee (October 2016)
and to 31 December 2016, there were taken a range of measures
and practical actions to ensure the activity was carried out and
the duties were fulﬁlled, as follow:
• Proposal of approval of the NBM’s general control by the
Supervisory Board.
• Meetings with the management and the NBM employees
during which information about their activity was shared.
• Meetings with the IMF representatives within the mission on
safety and credibility assessment of the NBM (Safeguards
assessment), during which opinions were shared on the role
and accountability of the Audit Committee, supervision of
audit mechanisms, ﬁnancial reporting and internal control
systems.
• Meeting with the external audit company representative, who
shared brief information on the external audit activity within
the NBM.
• Organizing of a joint meeting with the representatives of
USA Treasury, Technical Assistance Bureau of the NBM, where
opinions were shared about the activity regulation of the Audit
Committee and the Supervisory Board, access to information
for the members of the Audit Committee and the Supervisory
Board, international experience relating to the cooperation
between the management bodies of the central banks.
• Analysis
of
documents
concerning
the
IAD
activity
organization
and
monitoring
procedure,
including
the
monitoring of recommendations issued by the external audit,
in order to have the possibility to analyze fraud, ﬁnancial,
and image risk exposures, Strategic Plan/Internal Audit
Annual Plan and amendments made for 2017.
• Analysis of quarter-based synthesis reports issued by the
Internal Audit Department, progress of the enforcement of the
approved Internal Audit Annual Plan, and the progress related
to the implementation by the operational and executive
management of internal/external audit recommendations.
• Analysis of internal regulations which regulate the NBM
policies in the ﬁeld of ﬁnancial reporting, internal control, risk
management, internal and external audit.
During this period, 4 meetings of the Audit Committee were held
on the following topics:


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
107
• Establishing collaboration procedures with the head of
Internal Audit Department and Subunits relating to the
implementation of international audit standards, monitoring
and direction procedures of the internal audit function and
insurance and monitoring procedures of the external audit
activity’s independence.
• Budget, Finance, and Accounting Department informing the
members of the Audit Committee about the functionality and
activity of the department, accounting policy, the NBM’s
ﬁnancial
reporting
procedures,
implementation
of
international standards in the preparation of the ﬁnancial
situation. Subsequently, there were clariﬁed issued relating
to the elaboration, enforcement and reporting procedures of
the budget of the National Bank of Moldova, including the
cost estimate and allowances for investments.
• Informing the members of the Audit Committee about the
activity of the external audit, company’s activity in the ﬁeld
of the ﬁnancial situations audit according to IFRS provisions
in the central banks, organization and independence of the
audit company, summary of staff experience engaged in
activities with the NBM in the quality and compliance control
procedures with ISA, term of ofﬁce of the company on the
external audit of the National Bank of Moldova,
brief
description of audit results for 2015 and the external audit
plan for 2016. Measures relating to independent monitoring
and activity of the external audit.
• Analysis and coordination of the Internal Audit Plan for 2017
being submitted for approval to the Supervisory Board of
the NBM. Internal Audit Plan for 2017 was developed based
on risk assessment methodology, support-instruments as
follow: (i) Audit Universe; (ii) Risk assessment by process of
activity/activities; (iii) Analysis of incidents recorded in the IS
of the NBM (SGED); (iv) Fraud and corruption risk assessment;
(v) Risk mapping at the NBM level.
In its activity, the Audit Committee organized, when required,
working meetings with the general auditor and other employees
of the NBM in order to exercise its duties pursuant to the powers
granted by the Law on the NBM and the Regulation of operation,
participated in the elaboration of and ﬁled proposals to the
Executive Board and the Supervisory Board for the approval of the
Regulation on the operation of the Audit Committee of the NBM,
Regulation on the Internal Audit Department of the NBM and other
documents.
3.14
Legal and regulatory framework
amendment
Foreign exchange regulation
In 2016, the National Bank of Moldova (the NBM) passed a range
of legal framework-related amendments on foreign exchange
regulation.


108
Annual Report (BNM, 2016)
I. In order to implement the provisions of item 267 of the National
Action Plan for the implementation of the Association Agreement
between the Republic of Moldova and the European Union for
2014-2016 and to improve the legal framework elated to foreign
exchange sector, the Parliament of the Republic of Moldova passed
the Law no.94 of 13 May 2016 on amendment and addenda of the
Law no.62-XVI of 21 March 2008 on foreign exchange regulation.
The main amendments operated through this law refer to the
following aspects:
• gradual liberalization of some foreign exchange operations
that have previously been subject to authorization from the
NBM. In this regard, the following operations may be carried
out without the NBM authorizations from 10 June 2016: a
range of foreign exchange capital operations of residents in
amount of up to EUR 10 000; operations of purchase by
residents of ﬁnancial instruments issued by international
organizations; operations of purchase of foreign ﬁnancial
instruments by licensed banks and by entities whose activity
is regulated and supervised by the National Commission
for Financial Market; granting by licensed banks of ﬁnancial
loans to non-residents; import in /export from the Republic
of Moldova by non-resident banks of foreign exchange cash;
import in /export from the Republic of Moldova by resident
banks and non-resident banks of cash in national currency in
the amount that not exceeding MDL 100 000;
• implementation of notiﬁcations to the NBM about foreign
loans/credits received by residents within direct investment
operations and waiver of notiﬁcation about loans/credits and
guarantees received by residents from non-residents, the
amount of which does not exceed EUR 50000 (or its
equivalent);
• optimization of use of cash when carrying foreign exchange
operations, especially, for the consolidation of efforts of the
NBM and the Government to tighten the area of use of cash
and to promote cashless payments;
• introduction of some provisions concerning the appearance
on the ﬁnancial market of non-banking payment service
providers;
• supplement of provisions concerning the application of
safeguard measures, especially on systemic ﬁnancial crisis.
Most amendments carried out by Law no.94 refer to foreign
exchange entities. The most signiﬁcant amendments related to
the activity of these entities refer to:
• entitling foreign exchange entities to change, during business
hours, the exchange rate (pursuant to internally drafted
procedures), and providing the right to the NBM to set the
frequency of change of exchange rates, and to require from
foreign exchange entities to set the buy and sell exchange
rates for USD, EUR, GBP, RON, and UAH with two decimal
positions, while for RUB – with three decimal positions;


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
109
• entitling the customer of the foreign exchange entity to
request the repeal of foreign exchange operation performed
at the window within 30 minutes from the completion of
foreign exchange operation, at the same time complying with
certain conditions, such as the currency exchange entity’s
liability to return the funds concerned to the customer;
• allowing foreign exchange entities to carry out foreign
exchange operations in cash with individuals through foreign
exchange machines, with the conditions for use of such
machines;
• cancelling the obligation of the licensed banks and foreign
exchange entities to ensure, at the beginning and during the
business hours, the availability of an amount equivalent to
MDL 100 000 for every foreign exchange entity of the bank
and the equivalent of MDL 400 000 for foreign exchange
ofﬁce, including for each of its subsidiaries.
Amendments related to the licensing of foreign exchange ofﬁces
and hotels aim to change the validity term of the license from
5 years to indeﬁnite; to increase license fee for these entities;
to change the list of documents that are to be submitted to the
NBM for the NBM license application; to specify requirements to
shareholders/associates and managers of foreign exchange ofﬁces
and hotels pursuant in the context of the legislation on money
laundering and terrorism ﬁnancing; to specify the reasons for
rejecting license issuance.
Amendments made to the Law on foreign exchange regulation
aim to contribute to streamline control measures from the NBM
and to apply penalties to foreign exchange ofﬁces and hotels,
and to increase the level of responsibility of administrators and
shareholders/associates of foreign exchange ofﬁces in order to
comply with the licensing and activity conditions.
Thus, amendments made to the Law no.94 are targeted at
gradually removing the authorization requirement by the NBM of
the performance by residents
of capital foreign
exchange
operation, developing and making more efﬁcient the domestic
foreign exchange market, increasing the level of competition on
the foreign exchange market, strengthening the protection of the
rights of consumer-customers of foreign exchange entities,
improving legal framework related to foreign exchange units, and
optimizing statistics related to external liabilities.
II. In order to bring the regulatory acts of the NBM in line with
the Law no.94 of 13 May 2016, the NBM launched the process
of reviewing regulatory acts that govern the activity of foreign
exchange units and the regulatory framework concerning the
notiﬁcation and authorization of external commitments.
- By the Decision of the Executive Board of the NBM no.295 of 27
October 2016 the frequency of changing the exchange rates by
the foreign exchange entities at the beginning of the business
hours was set out (once or twice, depending on the duration of
regular business hours of the foreign exchange entity).
- Taking into account the fact that the primary rules of licensing,
control and penalization of foreign exchange entities are set out in
the Law on Foreign Exchange Regulation, the Regulation on


110
Annual Report (BNM, 2016)
licensing, control and penalization of foreign exchange entities
set some particular provisions related to the licensing of foreign
exchange ofﬁce and hotel’s activity of foreign exchange in cash
with individuals, control of foreign exchange entities carried out by
the
NBM,
the
application
of
penalties
to
them
and
the
communication of licensed banks to the NBM about foreign
exchange bureaus and foreign exchange machines.
Unlike previous rules, the regulation sets out new aspects
(disclosed
herein),
conditions,
especially
related
to
the
amendment of primary regulatory framework.
Thus, relating to licensing of foreign exchange ofﬁces and hotels
activity,
the application forms and other documents to be
presented to the BNM for the purpose of licensing have been
subject to amendment; there have also been set out the forms of
documents meant to conﬁrm certain aspects related to the use of
foreign exchange machines, forms of afﬁdavit of administrator,
deputy administrator, accountant of the foreign exchange ofﬁce or
hotel, and shareholders/associates of the foreign exchange ofﬁce.
The Regulation stipulated the deadline for annual submission (up
to 1 April) of criminal records and afﬁdavits of aforementioned
persons,
amended the list of documents that certiﬁed the
availability of funds required to obtain the license/certiﬁed copy of
the license by the foreign exchange ofﬁce,
adjusted other
provisions related to licensing process.
As to licensed banks, the new regulation sets out the notiﬁcation
form of the NBM by the licensed bank on the installation of the
foreign exchange machine and the amendment of data included in
the notiﬁcation concerned, at the same time, similar notiﬁcations
related to the foreign exchange bureau of the licensed bank being
adjusted.
• The Regulation provides a range of particularities related
to the control carried out by the NBM with reference to the
activity of foreign exchange entities. They have been brought
in line with the provisions of the Law on the National Bank of
Moldova and the Law on Foreign Exchange Regulation.
• As to the NBM applying penalties to foreign exchange entities,
unlike previous rules, the Regulation set out particularities
related to the application and undeniable charge of ﬁnes by
the NBM from foreign exchange ofﬁces/hotels.
• There were added provisions concerning the withdrawal of
license of the foreign exchange ofﬁce/hotel pursuant to other
cases than the penalty.
- The Regulation on the activity of foreign exchange entities sets
out a set of particularities related to the activity of foreign
exchange entities, as follow:
• particularities concerning the information displayed by foreign
exchange entities for individuals;
• some aspects related to exchange rate and commissions that
apply for carrying out foreign exchange operations in cash
with individuals;


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
111
• operations of foreign exchange entities within the activity of
foreign exchange in cash with individuals;
• the way of performing foreign exchange operations in cash
with individuals.
Unlike previous relevant provisions, the Regulation sets out a
range of rules (that predominantly aim to protect the rights of
consumers), as follow:
• the notice in new wording, meant to inform individuals about
the most important aspects related to the performance of
foreign exchange operations at the window;
• technical-related requirements on the display of exchange
rates
on
the
advertisement
board
meant
to
prevent
misleading the customers;
• information-related requirements that is to be displayed on
the body and the screen of the foreign exchange machine and
the rules that apply for the carrying out of foreign exchange
operations through the machine;
• order-related rules on the exchange rate and commission
applicable to individuals, including amid the right of foreign
exchange entities to change exchange rates;
• particularities relating to the carrying out of revocation
operations by foreign exchange entities (including the
template of application of foreign exchange operation
revocation, form of revoked operations registry);
• speciﬁcation that the exchange rate bulletin does not replace
the receipt and is not valid without receipt.
- The regulatory framework relating to reporting to the NBM by
foreign exchange entities was reviewed in 2016, in such a way
that the reports concerned reﬂect also the information on new
activity aspects of these units. The Instruction on reporting to the
National Bank of Moldova by foreign exchange ofﬁces and hotels
set out the reports related to the exchange rate activity of foreign
exchange ofﬁces and hotels, deadlines and form of presenting
the reports that would reﬂect the information about set exchange
rates and operations performed at the foreign exchange entity’s
windows and those carried out through foreign exchange machines,
and foreign exchange operations revoked by individuals. In this
context, the Instruction on reporting of some foreign exchange
operations by licensed banks was amended.
- A range of amendments were made to the Instruction on external
commitments aiming to bring them in line with the Law no.94
of 13 May 2016, to optimize payment/transfer procedure within
notiﬁed external commitments, and to report such commitments.
The most signiﬁcant are:
• adjustment
of
provisions
related
to
notiﬁcation
and
authorization of external commitments,
amendment of
annexes to notiﬁcations and authorizations;


112
Annual Report (BNM, 2016)
• notiﬁcation to the NBM about external loans/credits received
by the residents within the operations related to direct
investments pursuant to contracts signed after 10 December
2016, notiﬁcation and reporting procedures set out in the
instruction being applied;
• establishment of particularities related to the notiﬁcation
by the bank of external loans/credits without the NBM to
issue notiﬁcation annexes, the reporting of such loans/credits
shall be carried out pursuant to the Instruction of reporting
of some foreign exchange operations by licensed banks, that
has been duly amended;
• simpliﬁcation of payment/transfer procedure of notiﬁed
external commitments, by exempting the resident payment
service providers of the liability to request from customers to
ﬁll out the notiﬁcation annexes; this responsibility lies on the
residents who notiﬁed external commitments to the NBM.
- Among other amendments and addenda to the regulatory
framework in the ﬁeld of foreign exchange are:
• Amendments made to the Instruction on the presentation
by foreign exchange ofﬁces and hotels of electronic reports
to the National Bank of Moldova to bring its provisions in
line with the amendments to the regulatory framework on
reporting to the NBM by foreign exchange units.
• Amendments made to the Regulation on foreign exchange
entities to repeal the provisions that were reﬂected in the
new regulatory acts.
• Amendment of the Regulation on open foreign exchange
position in terms of relaxation of rules related to calculation
of open foreign exchange position applicable to off-balance
operation of buy/sale of foreign currency against MDL in
licensed banks, and between licensed banks and the NBM.
Prudential regulation
In order to maintain the domestic banking sector stable and to
ensure its development, a range of measures concerning banking
prudential regulation were taken during 2016.
I. By the Law on amendment and addenda of some legislative acts
no.182 of 22 July 2016, amendments and addenda were made to
the Law on Financial Institutions.
The
amendments
aimed
to
strengthen
the
national
legal
framework related to the activity of ﬁnancial institutions, notably,
the framework related to corporate governance, transparency of
shareholding, risk management and external audit activity in
ﬁnancial institutions.
The amendments were targeting the development of a healthy
corporate governance and management of risks according to
international principles in this sense, from the viewpoint of two
dimensions: (1) the banks independently shall evaluate and shall
develop internal procedures and systems to ensure that they are


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
113
adapted to the nature, size, and complexity of risks inherent to
the business model and activity conducted, and (2) the National
Bank of Moldova shall establish, through regulatory actsprinciples,
technical criteria and other requirements that are to be complied
with by banks.
At the same time, the regulatory framework that determined
certain obligations of external audit companies was established
through these amendments, including those of informing the NBM
about the actions that can lead to refusal of audit companies to
express their opinion on the ﬁnancial situation of audited banks.
Also, the right of the NBM to withdraw the acceptance issued to an
audit company was set out, provided that the latter fails to fulﬁll
the conditions for which it was accepted and/or does not comply
with the provisions of regulatory acts.
The
Law
on
Financial
Institutions
was
supplemented
with
provisions on certain deadlines related to the submission of set of
documents for persons elected or nominated as members of the
board, as well as additional requirements to bank administrators
whose conﬁrmation was withdrawn, including those who had been
informed that they would not be able to exercise any function in
the banking and non-banking sector for 10 years from the date of
conﬁrmation withdrawal.
In order to increase the discipline level of all banking market
actors, the penalties ceiling that the supervisory authority can
apply to banks,
administrators and bank shareholders was
increased, provisions related to holding liable persons that directly
contributed to the bank’s insolvency were set.
Thus, all bank
administrators who could be attributable to bank’s insolvency,
and any other person who contributed to the bank’s insolvency
status would be held liable.
II. Relevant amendments were made to the regulatory framework
subject to the legal framework concerning the banking activity
and the collateral.
These amendments and addenda aim to
increase
bank
shareholders’
transparency
through
early
intervention of the supervisory authority in the establishment of
the bank’s shareholding and to facilitate monitoring by the
supervisor of changes that take place in its structure.
In this regard, there were amendments in the Regulation on
holdings in the share capital of the bank related to the reduction
of substantial holding in the share capital of the banks from 5
percent to 1 percent, including to the permission of the National
Bank to acquire a holding, the assessment of the potential
acquirer by the banking supervisor, subsequent information of the
National Bank and permanent compliance of requirements on the
quality of the shareholding. Also, the level of holding in the banks’
share capital was amended, for which the National Bank’s
permission is required, with the inclusion of two new levels of 5
percent and 10 percent respectively. Simultaneously, there were
introduced prohibitions meant to order the formation of banking
holding according to legal provisions and to involve shareholders
in the efﬁcient management of their investments. To this end,
information
about
the
procedure
on
obtaining
preliminary
permission in writing from the National Bank of Moldova was
detailed, taking into account the nature of the permission applied
for by the person who intended to acquire, individually or in
concert, a holding in the share capital of the bank falling under


114
Annual Report (BNM, 2016)
the provisions of Article 15(2) of the Law on Financial Institutions
related to the suspension of the voting right and by the holder
whose substantial holding in the bank was intended to be set as
collateral.
The Regulation on the licensing of banks was also developed,
setting out requirements relating to the information that needed
to be presented by the bank should a decision be made during the
general meeting of shareholders about the increase of the share
capital of the bank by conducting an issuance of additional shares.
The Regulation on the voting right calculation and registration of
transfer of ownership on banks’ shares was supplemented with
details on the content of statement of activity in concert or lack
of activity in concert of the shareholder, with information on the
holding in the bank’s share capital, that would also include the
holding related to transfer of ownership on bank’s shares.
Amid the improvement of the regulation process related to the
bank’s
relationship
with
related
parties
and
taking
into
consideration the possibility of related parties to inﬂuence bank
governance, the list of persons deemed related to the bank was
broadened, thus contributing to the development of this concept
in line with the amendments to the Law on Financial Institutions
made through Law no. 233 of 3 October 2016 and international
experience in the ﬁeld.
At the same time, to identify bank’s related but non-declared
parties, the National Bank was entitled to use, during supervision,
certain identiﬁcation features to presume, until proven otherwise,
bank’s related parties. In this case, the bank shall prove in a well-
grounded and viable way that it has not violated the provisions of
the law in this regard.
Aiming to minimize the concentration risk of the bank’s exposure,
the limit of bank’s exposure to bank’s persons holding substantial
holdings and to related parties was set out in the Regulation
on large exposures. This limit refers to aggregated amount of
net debts to loans granted and contracted from the bank by the
shareholders who hold, or manage, less than 1 percent of the
bank’s share capital, including their related parties, and which
shall not exceed 20 percent of the regulatory capital of this bank.
To assume the risks on the violation of maximum limits of large
exposures and exposures to bank’s related parties, amendments
were operated stipulating that the bank’s regulatory capital,
according to the decision of the Executive Board of the National
Bank, will be decreased by the amounts exceeding the limits set
to aforementioned exposures.
In order to bring secondary regulatory acts in line with the Law on
Financial Institutions, a new version of the Regulation on banks’
holding in legal entities’ equity was approved, setting out the
mechanism and rules concerning the holding by the banks, directly
or indirectly, in the equity of legal entities, other than the licensed
banks of the Republic of Moldova.
In this regard, amendments were made referred to the amount of
holding of bank in the equity of legal entity that needs the National
Bank’s permission.
Thus, without permission in writing of the
National Bank, no bank, alone or within a group of persons acting


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
115
in concert, may hold in the legal entity’s equity either 20 percent
or more, or exceeding, according to its book value, 5 percent
from regulatory capital of the bank, or aggregately exceeding the
value of holdings and total book value 50 percent of the regulatory
capital of the bank.
Also, it was improved the process of obtaining this permission,
specifying the required information and documents and the
grounds for rejecting the application on the bank’s holding in the
legal entity’s equity in the context of remedial measures,
sanctions applicable to banks and intervention measures.
Pursuant to Law of Accounting no. 113-XVI of 27 April 2007, the
Regulation on disclosure by banks of the Republic of Moldova of
information
related
to
their
activity
was
amended
and
supplemented in order to specify the aspects that needed to be
considered when disclosing information about bank’s ﬁnancial
performances, including about economic and ﬁnancial activity,
management’s report, conditions of accepting deposits, and
bank’s governance.
To ensure an ongoing monitoring of banks and improvement of
banking supervision methods, amendments and addenda related
to obtaining by banks of information on compliance with liquidity’s
3rd principle were operated in the Instruction on drafting and
presentation
by
banks
of
reports
for
prudential
purposes,
requesting the presentation of a new report on liquidity on bands
of maturity (3rd principle) setting the requirement to liquidity,
using the following assets and debts’ bands of maturity: up to a
month, 1-3 months; 3-6 months; 6-12 months and over 12
months.
Furthermore, to give the supervisor a wider range of information
on banks’ activity, the information obtained from the banks and
related to the daily balance of synthetic accounts, placed/attracted
interbank funds and individuals’ deposits was broadened.
To streamline and update the bookkeeping in banks, Bookkeeping
chart of accounts in licensed banks from the Republic of Moldova
was amended.
In the virtue of the Law on Accounting no.113-XVI of 27 April
2007 and the Law on Public Funds and Budgetary and Tax
Responsibility no.181 of 25 July 2014, amendments and addenda
were made to accounts designated for record keeping of funds of
budget authorities/institutions and public institutions under
self-management.
At the same time, the purpose and description of calculated
interest accounts, revenue and expenses from interest to “Nostro”
and “Loro” accounts were amended by mutual addenda, which will
allow the disclosure of calculated interest to be received for “Loro”
accounts and interest to be paid on “Nostro” accounts.
Also, addenda were made to synthetic accounts related to funds
which, under legislation in force, are not subject to seizure, and
some amendments to bring the bookkeeping chart of accounts in
line with the legislation in force.


116
Annual Report (BNM, 2016)
Regulation on payment systems
According to Law on payment services and e-currency no. 114 of
18 May 2012, the National Bank of Moldova (the NBM) licenses,
regulates and supervises the activity of payment service providers
and e-currency issuers.
Pursuant to aforementioned law, one license for e-currency
issuance activity was issued in 2016.
To make the access on the market of payment services easier
and to simplify the registration process of payment operators of
various payment service providers, the Executive Board of the NBM
adopted the Decision no. 104 of 21 April 2016 on amendment and
addenda of Regulation on e-currency issuers’ and non-banking
payment service providers’ activity, approved by the Decision of
Council of Administration of the National Bank no.123 of 27 June
2013.
Some NBM’s regulatory acts were amended and supplemented
(the Regulation on loan transfer, approved by Decision of Council
of Administration of the NBM no.157 of 01 August 2013, the
Regulation on automated interbank payment system, approved
by the Decision of Council of Administration of the NBM no. 53 of
02
March
2006
and
the
Regulation
on
payment
service
providers’ activity in money transfer systems, approved by the
Decision of Council of Administration of the NBM no. 204 of 15
October 2010),
aiming to ensure the regulatory framework
relevant for exclusive implementation of IBAN codes when making
loan transfers in MDL, for the increase of the value limit for
payments made through MPay service, and the need to transpose
the
EU
norms
related
to
information
about
the
payer
accompanying the money transfer in the national legislation


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
117
3.15
The staff of the National Bank of
Moldova
and
its
professional
development
According to the 2013-2017 Strategic Plan of the National Bank of
Moldova, out of 10 strategic goals, 2 are related to human
resources,
as
follow:
optimization
of
human
resources
management and corporate culture development.
  
 
  
  
 
 
optimization 
of human 
resources
corporate 
culture 
development
Thus, key priorities of divisions dealing with human resources
development and management focused in 2016 on these ﬁelds.
The measures are detailed below.
Optimization of organization chart
Over 2016, more changes in the National Bank of Moldova’s
organization structure were carried out, meant to contribute to
the achievement of strategic goals of the bank and its basic tasks.
At the same time, these amendments aim to boost the NBM’s
capacity to adapt to changes in the economic environment by
redeﬁning
responsibilities,
restructuring
activities,
and
consolidating institutional capacities on the long term.
It should be noted that the structural changes did not occur as a
result of redundancy.
Below are several organization changes that were recently carried
out:
1. Strategic Planning and Organization Department has merged
with Human Resources Department with the purpose to increase
the NBM’s capacity for the development of human resources
management system.
2. 13 operating jobs have been created within Bank Supervision
Department, meant to strengthen work capacity, considering the
banking supervision workload and the need to implement latest
standards and requirements in the ﬁeld.
3.
The Executive Board of the National Bank of Moldova has
approved certain decisions that will come in force in 2017:
• establishment of new divisions, as follow: Financial Stability
Division, Bank Resolution Division, Money Laundering and
Terrorism Financing Division – all being created due to the
breakdown of Bank Supervision Department.
Thus, the
provisions of the Law on banks’ recovery and resolution and
Directive 2014/59/EU were complied with, by separating the
structure
performing
supervision
from
the
structure
performing resolution and ensuring operational freedom and
avoidance of conﬂict of interest. At the same time, these
changes are meant to strengthen the macroprudential
supervision and money laundering and terrorism ﬁnancing
capacity, within the bank’s legal responsibilities;


118
Annual Report (BNM, 2016)
• redeﬁnition of Payment System Department’s structure to
ensure separation of regulation function from control function
related to payment systems.
An important focus is the
development of payment systems and its merger with the
regulation function and the establishment, regulation and
supervision of single central Depository;
• establishment of Applied and Economic Research Department
by separating the Monetary Policy and Research Department,
the ﬁrst one being in charge for the research capacity building
of the NBM in various ﬁelds, depending on the NBM’s needs.
The availability of an ongoing research process in the central
bank is of major importance due to the increasing level of
uncertainty concerning world economy, occurrence of new
economic phenomena at national and regional level and
technical and scientiﬁc development of the society;
• establishment of Purchases, Contract Management and
Protocol
Division,
Technic-Administrative
and
Supply
Department and Communication and Mass Media Relations
Unit following the reorganization of existent subdivisions
(Administration and Purchases Department; Communication,
Mass Media and Protocol Unit), to strengthen institutional
capacity for public purchases, methodological coordination
and uniform assurance of activities relating to purchases of
goods, works and services, management of contracts and
protocol.

Statistical information
In 2016, staff ﬂow was of 11.37 percent, decreasing compared with
2015, when it recorded 12.61 percent and 2014 – when it was of
13.78 percent. By hierarchical levels, for management functions,
the staff ﬂow rate was of 5.73 percent, while for operating functions
– 13.78 percent.
The average age of the bank’s staff is 40.5 years. Almost 2/3 are
women (62.45 percent) and more than 1/3 (37.55 percent) are
men. At the level of heads of subunits, the ratio is 30/70 percent.
The average employment history in the NBM is of 10 years and
1 month. Most NBM employees have higher education – 82.49
percent, except technical staff.
There are 8 employees working in the bank who hold PhD degree
in economy, ﬁnance of technical sciences, which accounts for 1.69
percent, 102 employees holding Master Degree, which accounts
for 21.52 percent and other 281 employees with Bachelor Degree,
accounting for 59.28 percent.

human resources, over 40 percent or 177 employees participated

Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
121
3.16
Analysis of ﬁnancial situation for
2016
General considerations
Pursuant to the Law on the National Bank of Moldova no. 548-XIII
of 21 July 1995 (republished in the Ofﬁcial Monitor of the Republic
of Moldova, 2015, no.297-300, Article 554), the fundamental
objective of the National Bank of Moldova is to ensure and maintain
price stability.
The basic tasks of the National Bank are set out in the Law on
the National Bank of Moldova. According to legal provisions, the
National Bank of Moldova’s operations are carried out aiming
to promote and support state’s monetary and foreign exchange
policy, to ensure stability in the banking system, to issue national
currency, to manage foreign exchange reserves of the state, to
provide payment services and to issue e-currency.
Thus, the
maximization of proﬁt from the National Bank’s activity is not
a goal and cannot serve as a drawback in achieving the objective
and basic tasks.
Proﬁt available for distribution
The ﬁnancial result of the National Bank of Moldova depends
directly on the need to carry out currency and foreign exchange
operations, to achieve the fundamental objective and to fulﬁll the
Bank’s basic tasks. Evolution of proﬁt available for distribution
over 2014-2016 is shown in the chart 3.88.
As at 31 December 2016, the level of registered capital in total
currency liabilities accounted for 6.23 percent (on 31 December
2015: 8.88 percent).
Chart 3.88: Evolution of proﬁt available for distribution
(MDL, million)
1778.73
1960.89
-262.15
Source: NBM
In late 2016, the National Bank recorded a total loss in the amount
of MDL (262.15) million (in 2015, proﬁt available for distribution:
MDL 1 960.89 million), the main factors with major impact being:
• Decrease by MDL 2 048 million of revenue from foreign
exchange operations, following the slowdown of depreciation
of the exchange rate of MDL against USD from 25.89 percent
during
2015
to
1.64
percent
during
2016,
and
the
appreciation of exchange rate of MDL against EUR and GBP
by
2.74
percent
and
15.88
percent
respectively,
the
revenues coming predominantly from foreign exchange
sales.
• Increase of interest-related expenses on required reserves
by about MDL 238 million compared with 2015, following the
increase of the required reserve ratio in MDL and attracted
funds subject to reserve in MDL.


122
Annual Report (BNM, 2016)
During 2016, required reserve ratio in MDL accounted for 35
percent of the base, in 2015 – it was gradually increased from
14 percent of the base in January to 35 percent of the base in
September, maintaining to this level until the end of the year.
Required reserves ratio in foreign exchange, such as USD and EUR,
remained unchanged over 2015 and 2016, making up 14.0 percent
of the base. Required reserves base in MDL increased during 2016
by about 22.6 percent compared with late 2015.
• Increase of expenses from depreciation of discount on the
NBM certiﬁcates by about MDL 226 million compared with
2015, following the increase of daily average balance of NBC
which, during 2016 accounted for MDL 2 959 million (in 2015:
MDL 783 million). Average interest rate on NBC placed in
2016 was of 11.55 percent (in 2015: 15.45 percent).
• Decrease of revenue from interest on loans granted and repo
operations by about MDL 64 million, driven, mostly, by the
smaller volume of repo operations. Thus, in 2016, the volume
of liquidities supplied within repo operations accounted for
MDL 1 999 million (in 2015: MDL 9 690 million), and the
average interest rate was of 18.94 percent (in 2015: 14.89
percent).
• Revenues obtained from interest on state securities kept up
to maturity in the amount of MDL 151 million, representing
revenue from government bonds issued on 04 October 2016
in total amount of MDL 13 341.20 million for the enforcement
by the Ministry of Finance of payment obligations arising from
state guarantees. Government bonds with the maturity of
1-9 years were issued with the coupon rate of 1.40 percent
annually, and those with maturity of 10-25 years – with the
coupon rate of 5.30 percent annually.
• Decrease by about MDL 112 million compared with the same
period of 2015 of expenses for the print of domestic
currency,
including
its
shipping
and
storage
due
to
production of a smaller volume of domestic currency. Thus,
over 2016, expenses for the print of domestic currency,
including shipping and storage, amounted to MDL 6.96
million (in 2015: MDL 119.45 million).
• Increase by MDL 72 million of revenue from interest on
short-term deposits following an increase by 0.36 percentage
points of average value of deposit portfolio and long-term
deposit proﬁtability, accounting for 0.68 percent (in 2015:
0.32
percent),
and
of
overnight
deposit
proﬁtability
accounting for 0.21 percent (in 2015: 0.03 percent).
• Decrease by about MDL 31 million of expenses from foreign
exchange securities reassessment differences compared with
2015, following a smaller number of foreign exchange
securities reaching the maturity, positively reassessed in the
previous years, both of securities managed by the NBM and
the securities managed by the World Bank within the
Advisory and Investment Management Agreement, and the
decrease of losses recorded from the sale of securities.
The impact of events on the proﬁt available for distribution, by
ﬁelds of activity carried out by the National Bank of Moldova over
2016, is shown in table A.29.


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
123
The
impact
of
monetary
policy
implementation
and
activities concerning the relations with the Government
on the ﬁnancial situation of the National Bank of Moldova
During 2016, the National Bank of Moldova continued using
liquidity-absorbing instruments.
Expenses from sterilization
operations carried out through the issuance of certiﬁcates of the
National Bank of Moldova increased, accounting for about MDL
346 million, compared with MDL 120 million in 2015. The increase
of expenses on depreciation of NBC discount was driven by the
increase of daily average balance of the NBM certiﬁcates, which,
during the reporting period, accounted for MDL 2 959 million (in
2015: MDL 783 million).
Also, following the application of a higher required reserve ratio
in MDL during 2016, compared with 2015, and the increase of
attracted funds subject to reserving in MDL, the NBM recorded
expenses by about 42 percent higher.
The set of monetary policy facilities applied by the National Bank
of Moldova over 2016 and activities related to the relation with the
Government, generated a net loss of MDL 739.44 million, a double
value compared with losses recorded in 2015 (Table A.30).
Impact of state’s foreign exchange reserves management
operations on the ﬁnancial situation of the National Bank
of Moldova
State’s ofﬁcial reserve assets, held by the NBM, increased by 25.56
percent, or by USD 449.12 million (from USD 1 756.81 million to
USD 2 205.93 million) as at 31 December 2016 compared with 31
December 2015, being predominantly invested in USD, EUR, GBP
facilities. At the same time, an increase by 10.50 percent, or by
about USD 183.87 million (from USD 1 750.92 million to USD 1
934.79 million) in annual average volume of international reserves
was recorded during 2016.
Net revenue related to foreign exchange reserve management
and other foreign exchange operations accounted for MDL 630.36
million, decreasing by MDL 1 947.39 million compared with 2015,
when it was MDL 2 577.75 million.
The main factors that led to the decrease of revenue related to
foreign
exchange
reserve
management
and
other
foreign
exchange operations were:
• the slowdown from 25.89 percent during 2015 to 1.64 percent
during 2016 of exchange rate depreciation of MDL against
USD, and the appreciation of exchange rate of MDL against
EUR and GBP by 2.74 percent and 15.88 percent respectively,
the revenue being mostly recorded due to sale of foreign
exchange;
• the decrease of average value of securities portfolio and
securities
proﬁtability
issued
by
non-residents,
which
accounted for 1.37 percent (in 2015: 1.42 percent) during
the reporting year.


124
Annual Report (BNM, 2016)
The impact of foreign exchange reserves management operations
on the ﬁnancial situation of the National Bank of Moldova is shown
in table A.31.
The average proﬁtability rate related to foreign exchange reserve
management for 2016 decreased to 1.21 percent (in 2015: 1.34
percent), following the achievement of a lower proﬁt from a foreign
exchange conversion than the one recorded in 2015 (Chart 3.89).
Chart 3.89: Evolution of the foreign exchange reserves
proﬁtability in 2016 compared to 2015
0.68%

2016
2015
Source: NBM
At the same time, the decrease of net result recorded following
foreign exchange reserve management in 2016 compared with
2015 is mostly driven by the exchange rate of MDL against major
foreign currencies.
It should be noted that, pursuant to the best international practices,
and the Law on the National Bank of Moldova, the main criteria
to select reserve assets are the security of main amounts and
liquidity. Thus, only after the security of these two criteria, is the
proﬁtability of facilities in which state foreign exchange reserves
are invested taken into consideration.
The evolution of the ofﬁcial exchange rate of MDL against
currencies in which foreign exchange reserves are kept had a
signiﬁcant impact on the ﬁnancial situation of the National Bank of
Moldova. Its dynamic and annual average values can be seen in
table A.32.
Thus, in late 2016, MDL appreciation against currencies that made
up foreign currency supplies, and namely: against EUR by 2.74
percent and against GBP by 15.88 percent, led to unrealized
losses from exchange rate differences arising from foreign
exchange supply reassessment in amount of about MDL 1 033.31
million, and decrease in price of foreign exchange securities on
international markets led to unrealized losses recorded from
investment securities reassessment amounting to MDL 18.15
million. According to the Law on the National Bank of Moldova,
unrealized losses were covered from the reserve corresponding
accounts of unrealized revenues.
In late 2015, there were recorded unrealized revenue from
exchange rate differences arising from foreign exchange supply
reassessment in the amount of MDL 2 258.16 million and
unrealized losses from investment securities reassessment in the
amount of MDL 28.54 million.
Capital and reserves
The activities conducted by the National Bank of Moldova over
2016, to fulﬁll its main tasks, reﬂect on the bank’s balance sheet
structure. This fact is shown in table A.33.
Capital and reserves situation is reﬂected in table A.34.
Pursuant to Article 19 of the Law on the National Bank of Moldova
no. 548-XIII of 21 July 1995, the general reserve fund is exclusively
used to cover losses recorded according to the end of ﬁnancial
year’s results.


Chapter 3. The activitity of the National Bank of Moldova
125
Thus, the general reserve fund as at 31 December 2016 was used
to cover losses amounting to MDL 262 149 thousand recorded
in late ﬁnancial year, decreasing to MDL 1 543 791 thousand.
Respectively, the registered capital decreased to MDL 2 446 761
thousand.
As at 31 December 2016, bank’s capital and reserves summed up
MDL
8
985
541
thousand,
decreasing
compared
with
31
December 2015, pursuant to the coverage of unrealized losses
from exchange rate differences, arising from foreign exchange
supply reassessment in the amount of – MDL (1 033 306)
thousand and coverage of unrealized losses from investment
securities reassessment in the amount of MDL (18 146) thousand,
which had properly modiﬁed the respective reserve accounts.
Other reserves include revenue reserve from state securities
reassessment, recording a total positive result of MDL 4 660
thousand
and
positive
differences
from
monetary
gold
reassessment in amount of MDL 9 053 thousand.
As at 31 December 2016, registered capital’s level in total
monetary bonds accounted for about 6.23 percent (as at 31
December 2015: 8.88 percent).

142
Annual Report (BNM, 2016)
Table A.19: Investment limits and constraints applied by the NBM for investment risk management
Risks
Techniques to minimize the investment risk
Credit risk
* Investing in secure, high credit quality counterparts, selected based on the Standard&Poor, Moody and Fitch’s Ratings (the average rating
quoted by the above three agencies is to be used).
* Setting the minimum allowable rating for authorized counterparts’ transactions.
* Establishing investment limits depending on: the type, duration and rating of investment instruments, as well as the foreign exchange
composition.
* Daily monitoring of investment limits and compliance with credit limits.
* Quantiﬁcation of credit risk by using the default coefﬁcients determined by the Standard&Poor’s rating agency, for each type of
rating and term to maturity.
Market risk
* Daily market risk monitoring by assessing at market price the unrealized gains and losses incurred as a result of exchange rate
ﬂuctuations and developments in securities’ prices.
* Managing and monitoring the market risk of the investment securities portfolio by using the value-at-risk methodology (VaR and CVAR), which
estimates potential ﬁnancial losses based on a range of parameters and assumptions determined for different changes in market conditions.
* Monitoring the interest rate risk and determining (by using PV01) the sensitivity of the investment portfolio securities’ prices to interest
rate ﬂuctuations.
* Establishing by the NBM Executive Board of the ofﬁcial currency composition for the investment portfolio, that aims to cover foreign exchange
risk, the portfolio’s optimization through optimal proportioning of foreign exchange assets and liabilities, as well as by setting an investment
horizon to ensure the meeting of current external obligations and implementation of the monetary and foreign exchange policies of the
state. The ofﬁcial currency composition is determined based on an extensive analysis of: the foreign exchange composition of trade
in goods and services, the external debt, the intervention currency, etc. The reserves currency composition may deviate within
+/- 10% from the ofﬁcial currency composition.
* Establishing by the NBM’s Investment Committee of the currency composition of each sub-portfolio, and allowed deviations from it.
* Complying with the ofﬁcial currency composition and currency composition of each sub-portfolio.
Liquidity risk
* Securing liquidity through demand deposits and investing in securities with high liquidity and low risk.
* Ensuring daily liquidity risk monitoring by using market benchmarks set for indexed investment sub-portfolios grouped by instrument type
and foreign currency. The applied benchmarks are market indices that are recognized and used worldwide to assess the performance and
risks of investment portfolios.
* The NBM’s investment policy allows active reserves management to ensure limited instrument and duration deviation of +/- 20%
from the benchmark.
Source: NBM
